# gitmem solidity audit — September 2026 (GIT-113 Phase 0)

**Report only.** No source was changed, nothing merged, production untouched. Audited `origin/main` at `f93c3a3` (v1.11.0). Branch `audit/GIT-113-phase-0`.

**Method.** Seven pattern sweeps over all 114 non-test files in `src/` (33k lines), plus `bin/`, `hooks/` and `scripts/`: A silent failure, B false success, C schema assumptions, D environment assumptions, E concurrency, F tool/CLI contracts, G test blindness. Each sweep lists every instance with `file:line`, customer effect, reach and severity (sections 3–9). Community feedback was read from `community_feedback` via the service-role path and mapped to classes, tickets and regression tests (section 10).

**Verification.** The findings that lead the ranking were re-checked by hand, and the store-facing ones were probed read-only against the disposable venue (`vujndhcumgroztfkkdus`, a setup.sql store). The log is in section 11. Sweep claims that failed verification are corrected there, not silently dropped.

**Severity × reach.** Severity: *critical* = the user's memory is lost or a shipped feature is dead, with no signal; *high* = lost or wrong with a signal only on stderr, or wrong in a common setup; *medium* = degraded and visible, or wrong in a less common setup; *low* = cosmetic, cost or theoretical. Reach: how many customers hit it in normal use.

---

## 1. Ranked summary

| # | Finding | Class | Severity | Reach | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Remote recall calls an RPC no customer store has.** When the local vector index isn't ready (the first recall after every server start until warm-up finishes, a new or empty store, a failed bulk load, `GITMEM_SEARCH_MODE=remote`), recall calls `match_gitmem_learnings_weighted`. `setup.sql` (both versions) defines only `gitmem_scar_search`, which takes `similarity_threshold`, not `match_threshold`. A unit test pins the wrong name with a fetch stub. | C, F, G | critical | every Pro customer | `supabase-client.ts:693`; `setup.sql:322`; venue probe: **404 PGRST202** |
| 2 | **`init` seeds the memory store in `<cwd>/.gitmem`; the server and hooks read `~/.gitmem`.** A fresh install's starter scars, `config.json` (project), threads and payload template are never read, and the next `session_start` reports the new store as "found but NOT being read". | D, F | critical | every install via the wizard | `bin/init-wizard.js:25,169,397,440,498`; `gitmem-dir.ts:93-95` |
| 3 | **The auto-retrieve hook is dead on stock macOS.** It runs `timeout 2.5 node …`; macOS ships no `timeout`, the error is sent to `/dev/null \|\| true`, and the hook exits as "nothing relevant". CI's no-jq job runs on Linux, which has `timeout`. | D, G | critical | every Mac hook user without Homebrew coreutils | `hooks/scripts/auto-retrieve-hook.sh:174`; verified absent on the audit Mac |
| 4 | **`session_close`'s thread-sync gate is hollow.** `syncThreadsToSupabase` marks a thread synced after write helpers that swallow their own failures (`null` / `false` / `void`). So `all_synced` is true when every write failed: `threads.json` is pruned, session files and payload are deleted, and the display says COMPLETE. The GIT-70 test fails only the lookup, which is the one call that propagates. | A, B, G | high | Pro closes during any store trouble | `thread-supabase.ts:663-691` ← `:215-218, :267-270, :509-560`; `session-close.ts:1443-1450, 1689-1696` |
| 5 | **A failed refresh or flush empties recall.** `loadScarsFromSupabase` returns `[]` on any error; `flushCache` (run after every `create_learning`) re-initialises the index and `hook-scars.json` with `[]`, logs "N → 0", returns success, and `ensureInitialized` never retries. | A, E | high | Pro, any transient store error after a write | `startup.ts:225-227, 677-690, 80` |
| 6 | **One `currentSession` per process (GIT-103).** The desktop app serves every chat from one process, and MCP requests are not queued. Chat B's `session_start` takes over chat A's identity: A's scars, confirmations and threads land in B's session, and A's close clears B. | E, D | high | every desktop user with two chats | `session-state.ts:56`; `session-start.ts:1230`; `session-close.ts:581-608, 1677` |
| 7 | **`closing-payload.json` is one file per machine.** Two concurrent sessions read, overwrite or delete each other's closing reflection. | E | high | concurrent sessions (CLI + desktop, Cursor windows) | `session-close.ts:976-998, 1695`; `resolve-root.sh:44` |
| 8 | **`pruneStale` deletes live sessions:** any session over 24 h old regardless of its process, and, after one torn registry read, every other session's directory once 1 h has passed. The owner then no-ops every persist silently. | E | high | long-running sessions; multi-process | `active-sessions.ts:463-470, 107-111, 496-525`; `session-state.ts:407` |
| 9 | **`archive_learning` is broken on customer stores in two ways.** A short-prefix archive fails with `uuid ~~ unknown` (42883). A full-UUID archive of a row that doesn't exist reports `durable: true`, because all 8 `directPatch` callers ignore a 0-row result. | C, B, A | high | every Pro `archive_learning`; all PATCH paths | `archive-learning.ts:53-66, 133, 176`; venue probe: **42883** |
| 10 | **The free tier's only store can wipe itself.** Collections are rewritten whole and non-atomically, a corrupt file reads as `[]` ("starting fresh") so the next write replaces it, and read-modify-write is lock-free across windows. Same shape for `threads.json`. | A, E | high | free tier, crash mid-write or two windows | `local-file-storage.ts:37-66, 132-141`; `thread-manager.ts:249-272` |
| 11 | **Hooks track the wrong session, or none.** State is keyed on `$$` when `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` is unset (it is), so the nag and the Stop-hook meaningful-session test never accumulate. `recall-check` gates on the newest registry session, not this window's. `GITMEM_DIR` set in the MCP `env` block is invisible to hooks. | D, E | high | every hook user | `session-start.sh:170` et al.; `recall-check.sh:43-44`; D 9.1 |
| 12 | **Hostname is identity.** macOS renames the host (`.local`/`.lan`, `-N` suffix; the audit Mac is `-4`), after which sessions can't be resumed and registry slots are orphaned. | D | high | Mac laptops | `active-sessions.ts:320, 137-139`; `session-close.ts:1369` |
| 13 | **`session_close` displays "N scars applied ✓" from its inputs** before the fire-and-forget usage batch runs, and the batch reports `success: true, durable: true` with zero rows when identifiers don't resolve. `health` counts that as success too. | B, A | medium-high | every Pro standard close | `session-close.ts:378-425, 1606-1611, 1727`; `record-scar-usage-batch.ts:198-205` |
| 14 | **`absorb_observations` never persists on a customer store** but says "Absorbed N" and its description says "persist". The column is probed as absent mid-session and dropped again at close. | A, B, F | medium | every Pro user of the tool | `absorb-observations.ts:71-80`; `session-columns.ts:107-121` |
| 15 | **Failures render as empty results.** `search` and `log` show "No results found." / "No entries found." on any query error; `session_start` shows "No threads or decisions." plus the first-session welcome when Supabase is unreachable. | B, F | medium | any outage | `search.ts:218-230, 350-364`; `log.ts:283-294`; `session-start.ts:280-283, 1031-1034` |
| 16 | **`install-hooks` / `uninstall-hooks` destroy the user's other Claude Code hooks.** Install replaces `settings.hooks` wholesale when no gitmem hooks are present; uninstall deletes the whole key. | F | medium | users with their own hooks | `bin/gitmem.js:650-659, 805-807` |
| 17 | **Async licence validation can flip the tier to free mid-session,** after tools, config, storage and the session were set up as Pro. The session is opened in Supabase and closed to local JSON. | E | medium | licence failures (expired, device limit) | `server.ts:499-508`; `config.ts:141`; `storage.ts:143` |
| 18 | **Late session create can undo a close.** `createSessionRecord` (fire-and-forget) and `session_close` are both full-row merge upserts with no ordering; a late create reverts a closed row. GIT-73's wait covers metrics only. | E | medium | Pro, slow first write | `session-start.ts:444-457, 1149`; `session-close.ts:1550` |
| 19 | **Type and FK assumptions:** `action_protocol` / `self_check_criteria` arrays sent to TEXT columns; `open_threads` objects stored as JSON strings in `TEXT[]`; unguarded FKs to `gitmem_sessions(id)` on decisions, scar usage and threads. | C | medium | Pro, specific inputs | `create-learning.ts:119-121`; `session-close.ts:587`; `setup.sql:78, 176-177` |
| 20 | **v1.8.0's `refresh_scar_behavioral_scores` bumps `updated_at` on unchanged rows** at every `session_start`. Since GIT-98's per-row delta this costs only the scored rows (≈42 KB in the venue run), not the whole index, but the fix lives in a `setup.sql` customers never re-run. | C | low-medium | every v1.8.0 store with active scars | `setup-v1.8.0.sql:383-417` |
| 21 | **Tests are blind where the risk is.** 22 src files (5,706 lines) have no unit test; Supabase is real only in the tag-time venue gate, never on PRs; the session-close suites hand-build about 20 mocks each and today *reach* an omitted export (`getSessionDir`) that throws and is swallowed; the CLI/wizard e2e tests don't run in CI. | G | root cause | all of the above | section 9 |

**Counts across classes:** A 139 sites (1 critical, 6 high); B 7 false-success, 20 partial; C 17 findings beyond the 11 the GIT-109 analyzer already covers; D ~100 sites (4 critical); E 83 rows; F 30 tools and 17 CLI commands, 13 untrue description sentences; G 27 mock factories with reachable omitted exports.

**What the feedback says** (section 10): 13 of 21 user bug reports are class B, 7 are A, 5 touch D, none C or E. Every 1.8.0-era report is fixed in 1.9.0–1.11.0. Two fixed defects have no regression test: Pro embedding generation and `migrate-local`. Schema and concurrency failures aren't being reported because they are silent, which is class A.

---

## 2. Proposed order for Phases 1–6

Effort is engineer-days, including tests and the venue check, for one agent working alone.

| Phase | Theme | Contents (summary rows) | Why this position | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Stop live loss and dead features** (patch release) | #1 recall RPC: call `gitmem_scar_search` with its parameter names, and replace the pinned-name test with one derived from `setup.sql`. #2 `init` writes to the resolved root. #3 hook `timeout` fallback (portable bash `timeout` or `node` with its own timeout). #4 thread sync inspects helper results; add a test that fails the *write*. #5 a failed load never replaces a non-empty index or hook cache. #9 prefix archive via a UUID range filter, and every `directPatch` caller checks row count. #16 hooks merge/filter like the wizard does. | Small, local changes that each fix active data loss or a dead feature for every customer. All verifiable on the venue today. | 4–5 |
| **2** | **Test realism** (before any refactor) | Run the venue `flow` on every PR, or a PostgREST + Postgres container in CI loaded with v1.8.0 `setup.sql`. Shared mock fixtures that spread `importOriginal`, plus a lint that fails on a factory omitting an export the subject imports. Run the CLI/wizard e2e in CI. A macOS runner for `hooks/tests`. Regression tests for Pro embedding generation and `migrate-local` (feedback 54cb480a, c6e4cdfc). | Phases 3–5 rewrite the session and store core; without a real store and honest mocks on PRs they would regress silently, as #4 and the `getSessionDir` swallow show today. | 5–7 |
| **3** | **Honest results everywhere** (class B/F) | `durable`/`stored_in` reach the display for every write tool (server contract). #13: close awaits or labels the usage batch, and a zero-row batch is not success. #14: `absorb_observations` says "session only" where the column is absent. #15: `search`, `log` and `session_start` distinguish an outage from empty. `cleanup_threads` / `archiveDormantThreads` errors. The 13 untrue description sentences. `session_close`'s advertised schema. | One contract, applied mechanically once tests can see the store. Directly addresses the dominant feedback class. | 4–6 |
| **4** | **Store integrity and concurrency** | #10: atomic temp+rename for every JSON store and `session.json`; a corrupt file is quarantined, not treated as empty; locked read-modify-write for `threads.json` and the free-tier collections. #8: `pruneStale` by liveness, not age or registry presence. `file-lock` TOCTOU and timeout behaviour. #17: licence flip applies only at startup. #18: close waits for the pending session create. | Local-file correctness is independent of the session model, and Phase 5 depends on it. | 4–5 |
| **5** | **Multi-session model (GIT-103)** | #6: session state keyed by session (per MCP request/connection), not a module global. #7: payload path per session (`sessions/<id>/closing-payload.json`, with the hook text and GIT-99 path message updated). #11: hooks keyed on a stable session id. #12: identity not tied to hostname. The registry slot model revisited. | The largest change, touching `session-state`, start, close, recall, confirm and the hooks. It needs Phases 2 and 4 underneath it. | 7–10 |
| **6** | **Schema contract hardening** | Extend the GIT-109 analyzer to RPC names and parameters, select strings, row properties and `setup.sql` function bodies. #19: array→TEXT coercion at the write, FK guards (the GIT-73 pattern) for decisions, usage and threads. #20: client-side mitigation for the v1.8.0 refresh function. Tier-gate transcript dispatch. Literal table names through `getTableName`. Remove dead code (`captureSessionTranscript`, `semanticSearch`, `cachedListWins`, `telemetry`). | Mostly lower severity once #1 is fixed; the analyzer extension keeps it fixed. | 3–4 |

**Total ≈ 27–37 engineer-days.** If Chris wants the fastest reduction in customer harm: Phase 1 alone clears four of the five critical/high items that hit every customer.

---

## 3. Class A — Silent Failure Audit (gitmem v1.11.0, f93c3a3)


Scope: every non-test file under `src/` (114 files, ~33k lines). Read-only.
Method: brace-matched extraction of all 236 `catch` blocks + grep for `.catch(`, `void `, `Promise.all(`, write calls (`directUpsert|directPatch|upsertRecord|.upsert(|writeFileSync|fetch POST/PATCH`), then each candidate read in context.

Conventions used in the table:
- **Pattern**: 1 empty/comment-only catch; 2 catch-and-log-only; 3 `.catch(()=>{})`; 4 fire-and-forget / un-awaited promise; 5 `Promise.all` sibling-discard; 6 unchecked store write.
- **Tracked?**: `ET` = wrapped in `getEffectTracker().track(...)` so failure appears in `health`; `result` = failure reaches the tool's return value (flag/error/display); `stderr` = console only (invisible in MCP clients); `none` = nothing.
- **Reachable**: "yes" = ordinary Pro session; "only-if" = needs a specific store/network/fs failure.
- Severity per brief: critical = data the user thinks is saved is lost with no signal; high = lost, signal only on stderr; medium = degraded but visible; low = cosmetic/cache/telemetry.
- Free-tier-only or CLI-only (`src/commands/*`) sites are included for completeness but marked as such.

Rows excluded on purpose (not silent-failure sites): catch blocks that (a) rethrow, (b) return an explicit error/`success:false`/`notStored()` result to the tool caller, or (c) guard a pure read of an optional file and return a documented default. They are counted in the appendix but not tabled.

---

---

### (b) Top 10 worst instances

1. **#64 absorb-observations.ts:71-80 — critical.** On every store provisioned from setup.sql the `task_observations` column does not exist, so the `.then(present ? directUpsert : undefined)` chain resolves `undefined` with no log, no ET entry and no result flag; session_close's column filter then strips the same field. The tool reports "Absorbed N observations" and none are ever persisted anywhere durable for a Pro customer.
2. **#78 thread-supabase.ts:676 ← 215-218 — high.** `syncThreadsToSupabase` ignores `createThreadInSupabase`'s `null` and marks the thread synced; the close returns `all_synced:true`, and session-start.ts:873-880 later discards the local-only copy as "stale" after 24h. A thread lost with one stderr line, on the exact path GIT-69 was meant to close.
3. **#79 thread-supabase.ts:684 ← 267-270 — high.** Same loop ignores `resolveThreadInSupabase`'s `false`; the resolution never reaches Supabase, and because `all_synced` is true, threads.json is pruned — the only record of the resolution is deleted while the durable store still says open.
4. **#38 session-close.ts:1606-1611 — high.** The whole session's scar_usage batch is fire-and-forget; a `{success:false}` from the store is recorded by ET but the close has already printed `scars_applied: N`. On Pro this is the only path that writes per-session scar usage, so the input to blindspots/behavioral decay silently vanishes.
5. **#17 resolve-thread.ts:212-218 — high.** Cascade resolve of the "duplicate of" original: return value ignored, `.catch(() => {})`, and the result lists it under `also_resolved` regardless — a success claim for a write that may not have happened.
6. **#110 local-file-storage.ts:43-46 — high (free tier).** A corrupt `learnings.json` (or any collection) is read as `[]` "starting fresh" and the next `upsert` rewrites the file with one record; `writeCollection` is a non-atomic `writeFileSync`, so a crash mid-write manufactures exactly this file. Whole free-tier collections gone, write reports success.
7. **#85 thread-manager.ts:257-259 — high (free tier).** Same shape for threads.json, the free-tier thread SOT: corrupt → `[]` → next `saveThreadsFile` overwrites.
8. **#32 session-close.ts:1478-1481 — medium, but universal.** Every setup.sql store lacks `transcript_chunks`, so `storeHasTable` is false, the transcript is skipped, `transcriptStatus` stays undefined and the close display prints nothing. Customers never learn transcripts are not being captured.
9. **#20 archive-learning.ts:133-136 — medium.** `directPatch` on a full UUID that does not exist (no existence check on that input path) or an RLS-filtered row returns `[]`; the tool answers `success:true, durable:true`. Same "0 rows patched = success" defect at all 8 `directPatch` call sites.
10. **#47 session-start.ts:1148-1152 — medium.** The session row INSERT is fire-and-forget; a failure is stderr-only and the session_start result carries no flag, so a broken store is discovered only at close (or masked, because close recreates the row from the local file).

Honourable mentions: #11 (batch insert `Promise.all` misreports partial success and invites duplicate rows on retry), #74/#75/#94 (confirm/reflect/surfaced-scar persistence swallows fs errors, so a restart erases the session's scar bookkeeping), #105 (`transcript_path` patch warning is dropped by the only live caller), #41 (stale closing-payload.json leaks into the next session's close).

---

### (c) Pattern-level observations

1. **`recordMetrics(...).catch(() => {})` — 20 sites** (every tool). Benign: `recordMetrics` wraps its upsert in `getEffectTracker().track("metrics", …)` and never rejects on store errors, so the `.catch` is dead; failures do reach `health`. Free tier no-ops.
2. **Effect tracker coverage is real but late.** 14 tracked fire-and-forget sites (+2 internal) do land in `health` and in `formatWriteWarnings` at close. Two caveats: (i) the close display is built before the tracked promises started in that same close resolve (#33, #38), so their failures appear only in a later `health` call or the next close; (ii) ET is in-memory — a restart erases it.
3. **Every `directPatch` caller ignores the returned row array (8/8).** `directPatch` throws on HTTP errors but returns `[]` for a filter that matched nothing (RLS, wrong id, row not created yet). archive-learning, thread resolve/touch/archive, session embedding, superseded marking, transcript_path and relevance updates all report success on 0 rows.
4. **`syncThreadsToSupabase` (thread-supabase.ts:660-700) catches its own loop exceptions and records them in `failed[]`, but its three write helpers swallow errors internally and return `null`/`false`/`void`, which the loop never inspects.** The `failed[]`/`all_synced` machinery added for GIT-69 therefore only fires on exceptions the helpers do not already eat (e.g. the `directQuery` existence check), not on the writes themselves (#78, #79, #82).
5. **"log and return a default" is the dominant style in services (~36 catches).** `thread-supabase.ts` (8), `session-start.ts` loaders (5), `startup.ts` (3), `active-sessions.ts` (3) all return `null`/`[]`/`{count:0}` on failure, so callers cannot tell "store said nothing" from "store unreachable". Downstream this produces the first-session banner for a returning user (#43), `archived_count: 0` for a failed archive (#68), and aggregation fallbacks that silently show stale data (#70, #81).
6. **Capability probe "unknown" collapses to "absent" (store-columns.ts:36-38).** Correct for schema errors, but a transient network/auth error during the probe now silently drops optional columns from a write on a store that has them (#107), skips the transcript upload (#32) and skips observation persistence (#64). Only #107 logs; #64's skip path logs nothing.
7. **Local session-state persistence is best-effort everywhere** (session-state.ts:281/308/335/411, confirm-scars.ts:156, reflect-scars.ts:134, session-start.ts:621/836/1221/1446, active-sessions.ts:387/417). Each site is individually low-stakes, but together they mean an MCP restart can erase recall_called, recall_failure, surfaced scars, confirmations and reflections with only stderr lines — and the GIT-93 "failed recall ≠ empty recall" guard (#92) is one of the casualties.
8. **Free-tier store files are read with "corrupt → empty" and written non-atomically** (local-file-storage.ts:43-66, thread-manager.ts:249-272). Two independent sites produce the same wipe-on-next-write outcome (#85, #110). `active-sessions.ts` and `vector-disk-cache.ts` already have tmp+rename helpers; the SOT files do not use them.
9. **Setup.sql gaps are handled by silent skipping rather than disclosure.** `transcript_chunks` (#32), `task_observations` (#64), `archived_at` (archive-learning.ts:129-136), `refresh_scar_behavioral_scores` RPC (#58), variant tables (dev-gated). Each skip is by design, but none is reflected in the affected tool's result, so a customer cannot tell a disabled feature from a broken one.
10. **`Promise.all` is mostly safe here** — 7 of 9 sites wrap loaders that self-catch and cannot reject. The two that can reject are record-scar-usage-batch.ts:175 (loses sibling ids, invites duplicate rows on retry, #11) and analyze.ts:136 (visible failure). session-start.ts:190 and variant-generation.ts:320 already use `allSettled`.
11. **Dead code with silent-failure shape:** `captureSessionTranscript` (session-close.ts:645-735) is never called; its `.catch(err => console.error)` and the `patch_warning` propagation it contains do not run. The live inline path (1482-1501) re-implements it without the `patch_warning`/`{success:false}` logging.
12. **Result-level honesty (GIT-101 `writeResult`) is applied to the primary write but not to the secondary effects it triggers.** create_learning/decision/thread, resolve_thread, archive_learning, session_close all return an honest `WriteResult` for their main row, then launch 1-4 tracked or untracked side writes whose failure never changes that result. The single exception is create_thread, which reads back its row before claiming success.
13. **Two error-shape inconsistencies worth noting (not tabled, visible failures):** create-decision.ts:150-160 returns `display: "Failed to log decision"` with the cause only on stderr (create-learning includes it); the free-tier recall catch (recall.ts:495-511) returns the error but does not call `setRecallFailure`, unlike the Pro path (766).

---

## 4. Class B — False Success audit (gitmem v1.11.0, f93c3a3, branch audit/GIT-113-phase-0)


Read-only audit. Scope: every handler in src/tools/*.ts plus src/services/* that write to a store.
Dispatch fact (src/server.ts:429-430): when a result has a string `display`, ONLY that string is sent to the client. Result fields (success/durable/stored_in) never reach the agent when a display exists.

### Working notes (incremental — table at end)

#### Store primitives (baseline for every finding)
- src/services/supabase-client.ts:418-462 `directUpsert`: throws on non-2xx (L440-443) AND on empty representation (L451-456). Honest — a resolved promise means a row exists.
- src/services/supabase-client.ts:476-510 `directPatch`: throws on non-2xx (L505-508) but RETURNS `[]` when the filter matched zero rows (L510). Any caller that discards the return value cannot distinguish "updated" from "no such row".
- src/services/local-file-storage.ts:49-67 `writeCollection` uses fs.writeFileSync — throws on I/O failure, so free-tier `upsert` (L128-143) is honest. Note L58-64: >10MB silently drops oldest 20% of records and returns normally.
- src/services/effect-tracker.ts:136-178 `track()`: never rethrows; rejection -> returns undefined; `{success:false}` result -> recorded as failure but result still returned. Only visible via `health`.
- src/services/triple-writer.ts:288-347 `writeTriples`: throws if any triple failed (so tracker records it). Callers all use track() → invisible to tool result.
- src/services/write-health.ts:217-224: an unexpected error in the probe returns `{ ok: true, mode: "skipped" }` — summary says "durability UNVERIFIED" yet ok:true (health line prints "(ok, ...)").
- src/services/write-result.ts:36-45 `writeResult(durable, storedLocally)`: pure function of its args — honesty depends entirely on what each caller passes as `durable`.

#### Service-level findings (thread + suggestion stores)
- src/services/thread-suggestions.ts:206-220 `saveSuggestions`: try/catch swallows fs.writeFileSync errors, logs to stderr, returns void. Callers (dismiss_suggestion, promote_suggestion) cannot know the file was not written.
- src/services/thread-supabase.ts:725-771 `archiveDormantThreads`: catch at L767-770 returns `{archived_count: 0, archived_ids: []}` on ANY error — including an error on the Nth PATCH after N-1 rows were already archived (L755-758 loop). Partial archivals are reported as zero. Callers cannot distinguish "nothing eligible" from "store failed".
- src/services/thread-supabase.ts:573-716 `syncThreadsToSupabase`: the per-thread try block calls `createThreadInSupabase` (L674) which RETURNS NULL on failure (L215-218, never throws), `resolveThreadInSupabase` (L682) which RETURNS FALSE on failure (L267-270), and `touchThreadsInSupabase` (L671, L689) which swallows every error (L561-564). None of these return values are examined; `synced.push(thread.id)` at L691 runs regardless. Therefore `failed` can only be populated by the directQuery lookup at L657 throwing. `all_synced: true` (L712) is reported when every create/resolve/touch failed. The GIT-69 comment (L578-582) says this gate exists so session_close can refuse to prune the local cache — the gate is hollow for write failures.
- src/services/thread-supabase.ts:226-271 `resolveThreadInSupabase`: returns true after directPatch (L264) without inspecting the returned row array; row was looked up at L240 so a zero-row patch is only a race. Minor.
- src/services/thread-supabase.ts:198-219 `createThreadInSupabase`: honest (null on failure) — caller responsibility.
- src/services/store-columns.ts:32-39: a probe that fails for a non-schema reason (network down) returns "unknown" → column treated as absent → optional field silently dropped from the write. By design (comment L17-19); noted because it means absorb_observations persists nothing when the store is unreachable and says nothing.

#### Tool notes batch 1 (create_thread, resolve_thread, promote_suggestion, archive_learning, record_scar_usage, record_scar_usage_batch)
- create_thread (src/tools/create-thread.ts): honest. durable = supabaseSynced && post-write read (L363-367); display derived from `durable` (L397-405); success = durable || !hasSupabase() (L408). Only nit: L313 `if (threads.length > 0)` — the in-memory session thread list is NOT updated when it is currently empty, so the first thread created in a session is not in session state (affects session_close's thread sync input, not this tool's claim).
- resolve_thread (src/tools/resolve-thread.ts): primary outcome honest via `writeResult(supabaseSynced, true)` L283 and display rewrite L284-290. GAP (partial): cascade resolves L212-218 — `resolveThreadInSupabase` return ignored, `.catch(()=>{})`; display L280 says "Also resolved: t-xxx" regardless of whether the durable store accepted the cascade. Also L246/L259 hard-code `project: "default"` for triples (not a success claim; noted).
- promote_suggestion (src/tools/promote-suggestion.ts:96-97,107): after createThread succeeds, `saveSuggestions(suggestions)` swallows write errors (thread-suggestions.ts:206-220); display "Promoted suggestion to thread\nID: t-xxx" and success/durable copied from createThread (L101-103). If suggested-threads.json write fails the suggestion stays "pending" and re-surfaces at next session_start while the tool said promoted. Partial gap (the thread itself IS honest).
- archive_learning (src/tools/archive-learning.ts): FALSE SUCCESS on pro. L133-136 `await directPatch(learningsTable, { id: eq.<id> }, {...})` — return value (row array) discarded. directPatch returns [] for zero matched rows (supabase-client.ts:510). L47-51: a full-UUID input bypasses the existence lookup entirely. L176 `...writeResult(hasSupabase())` → {success:true, durable:true, stored_in:"supabase"}; display L173 "Archived learning <id>.\n(Nms)". So archive_learning(<any well-formed UUID that does not exist, or a row RLS hides>) reports durable success. cache_flushed (L138-143) IS derived from the real flushCache outcome — honest.
- record_scar_usage (src/tools/record-scar-usage.ts): honest — directUpsert throws → catch → notStored (L110-121). writeResult(hasSupabase()) at L105 is reached only after a successful await.
- record_scar_usage_batch (src/tools/record-scar-usage-batch.ts): no `display` → JSON reaches agent. (a) L101-115 free tier returns `success: true, durable: false, stored_in: null` — contradicts write-result.ts contract ("success false whenever nothing was stored") — by design per comment, but a caller at free tier is told success with zero usage_ids. (b) L167-176: Promise.all — one failed upsert rejects the whole batch → catch L206 reports notStored with `usage_ids: []` although the other upserts already landed (under-report, not false success). (c) L65-83 partial-title resolution scans an unordered `limit: 100` page and matches `description.includes(identifier)` — can attribute a usage to the wrong scar and report it resolved.

#### Tool notes batch 2 (create_learning, create_decision, save_transcript, confirm_scars, reflect_scars)
- create_learning (src/tools/create-learning.ts): honest. writeResult(hasSupabase()) L318 reached only after awaited directUpsert (L222) with id check (L233-238); free tier awaited local upsert (L284); catch → notStored (L330-337). Side effect not in claim: flushCache L277-279 fire-and-forget — on failure the new learning is invisible to recall until restart, display still says "Created scar". Triples/variants via tracker (health only).
- create_decision (src/tools/create-decision.ts): honest; same structure (L87 awaited, L145 writeResult, L154-159 notStored). Failure display L158 "Failed to log decision" drops the error message (result.error not set at all) — the agent is told it failed but not why.
- save_transcript (src/tools/save-transcript.ts): honest. `writeResult(true)` L113 only after `supabase.saveTranscript` resolved; uploadFile throws on non-2xx (supabase-client.ts:830-833). patch_warning L118 surfaces the session-row PATCH failure. No display → JSON reaches agent. Nit: on free tier isConfigured() is false → uploadFile throws → notStored (fine), but if a free-tier user happens to have SUPABASE_URL set, writeResult(true) at L113 would yield {success:true, stored_in:"local"} for a Supabase upload — label wrong, not a false success.
- confirm_scars (src/tools/confirm-scars.ts): does NOT call record_scar_usage (session_close does). Claims: L103 "SCAR CONFIRMATIONS ACCEPTED", L110 "All recalled scars addressed. Consequential actions are now unblocked." Evidence: in-memory addConfirmations (L310) — always succeeds. `persistConfirmationsToFile` L140-159 swallows fs errors and silently returns when session.json is absent (L146). If the PreToolUse hook reads .gitmem/sessions/<id>/session.json (see gitmem-dir/hook), "unblocked" can be false while the display says it. Partial gap; GIT-93 recall-failure branch L196-220 is honest.
- reflect_scars (src/tools/reflect-scars.ts): same shape; L92 "Session close will use these for execution_successful." persisted in memory (L249) + file with swallowed errors (L122-137). Reflections reach session_close via in-memory getReflections — true within one process; false across an MCP restart if the file write failed. Partial.

#### session_close (src/tools/session-close.ts) — detailed
- Session row itself: HONEST. L1548-1553 awaits directUpsert; `sessionRowStored = true` only after (L1553); catch L1739-1758 returns writeResult(sessionRowStored) + success:false.
- SCAR USAGE — FALSE SUCCESS (confirmation before completion). L1606-1611 `getEffectTracker().track("scar_usage", ..., () => recordScarUsageBatch(...))` is fire-and-forget. Display built at L1727 via formatCloseDisplay: L378-379 "N scars applied" and L419-425 one `STATUS.pass` line per scar — all computed from `params.scars_to_record` (input), never from the batch result. close_compliance.scars_applied (buildCloseCompliance) likewise from input. The batch has not resolved when the display is rendered, so formatWriteWarnings (L440) cannot include it either. Compounding: recordScarUsageBatch returns success:true even when every identifier failed to resolve (failed_identifiers populated, usage_ids []) — effect-tracker.reportedFailure (effect-tracker.ts:103-113) only flags `success === false`, so `health` also counts it as a success. Net: on a customer store where gitmem_scar_usage is missing/blocked or scar ids don't resolve, close says "✓ complete · 3 scars applied" with three check marks and nothing anywhere records otherwise.
- THREAD SYNC — FALSE SUCCESS. L1423 `threadSync = await syncThreadsToSupabase(...)`; L1443 `if (!threadSync.all_synced)` skip prune else L1449-1450 `saveThreadsFile(openThreadsOnly)` (drops resolved threads locally); L1687 `partialPersist = !threadSync.all_synced`; L1732 `success: !partialPersist`. But all_synced is hollow (thread-supabase.ts:663-691: create→null, resolve→false, touch→swallowed, none examined, synced.push unconditional). So: every thread create/resolve fails on the store → all_synced:true → threads.json pruned → cleanupSessionFiles + payload deleted (L1689-1696) → display `STATUS.complete` with "N threads". This is the exact scar (cd345431) the GIT-69 comment at L1401-1408 says was fixed. Only a *lookup* failure (directQuery at thread-supabase.ts:657 throwing) populates `failed`.
- TRANSCRIPT — hidden failure, no false claim. L1459 `transcriptStatus` is declared and never assigned on the pro path (upload is tracked fire-and-forget L1482-1502), so formatCloseDisplay's transcript-failure line (L434-437) can never render. `captureSessionTranscript` (L645-733), which did set it, is unreferenced (verify with grep). Failure visible only via `health` later.
- "N learnings" (L380) and close_compliance.learnings_stored come from `params.learnings_created.length` — the agent's claim, not a store read. Display restates input as outcome. Partial.
- EMBEDDING/SUGGESTIONS (L1556-1598) and updateRelevanceData (L1622): fire-and-forget, nothing claimed in display → no false claim; failures reach `health` only. saveSuggestions inside (L1593) swallows.
- FALSE FAILURE (mismatch): L1681 `await writeAgentBriefing(...)` runs after the upsert; if it throws, the catch reports display `STATUS.failed` + "Failed to persist session: <briefing error>" while the row is stored (writeResult(sessionRowStored) → success overridden to false at L1751) and session state already cleared (L1677). Agent is told the close failed; retry will find close_compliance already set.
- Write warnings (L440, formatWriteWarnings L471-487): computed from tracker at render time — reflects failures from EARLIER in the process (create_learning triples etc.), never the writes this call just launched.
- Free tier `sessionCloseFree` L223-344: honest (awaited local upserts; catch → notStored + STATUS.failed display).

#### Remaining tool notes (session_start, session_refresh, recall, read-only tools, index_docs, absorb/dismiss/feedback/cleanup confirmations)
- session_start (src/tools/session-start.ts): session row write is fire-and-forget L1149-1152 (`registerPendingSessionRow(sessionId, createSessionRecord(...).then(r => r.stored))`); `stored` is consumed only by metrics.ts:156-178 (FK guard). Display built L1300 → productLine("active") + session id, before the write resolves; nothing in display or result reports a failed row. loadLastSession catch L280-283 and the allSettled halves L204/L211 return empty → display L1031-1034 "No threads or decisions." identical to a genuinely empty store; L1299 `isFirstSession = !isResuming && !slimLastSession` → a Supabase outage prints FIRST_SESSION_NUDGE ("Welcome to gitmem...") to an established user. writeSessionFiles: session.json write swallowed L836-838; whole call wrapped L1219-1223 with warn only. `resumed` (L1256) comes from disk/registry (checkExistingSession) — honest.
- sessionStartFree: local session upsert failure swallowed L596-598; display still "active".
- session_refresh (L1329-1504): same loadLastSession semantics; display "refreshed"; writeSessionFiles failure swallowed L1446-1448. Free tier returns `message` without display (JSON) — honest.
- recall (src/tools/recall.ts): honest post-GIT-93. Local index not ready → Supabase fallback L544-565; any throw → setRecallFailure + "⚠️ Error querying institutional memory" L758-783; confirm_scars distinguishes (confirm-scars.ts:196-220). local-vector-search.ts:220-223 returns [] when not ready but recall guards with isLocalSearchReady() first. Residual: local index staleness (create_learning flush failure L277-279; another process's writes until periodic refresh) makes "No relevant scars found" (L192) possible while the scar exists — read-side, low.
- search (src/tools/search.ts): catch L218-230 (free) and L350-364 (pro) return `display: buildSearchDisplay([], 0, ...)` → "No results found." (L92). An unreachable store is rendered as an empty corpus. log (src/tools/log.ts): identical, L191-200 and L283-294 → "No entries found." (L75).
- list_threads (src/tools/list-threads.ts:100-150): Supabase null → session aggregation → local file; display L56 "N open · M resolved" never states the source, so a degraded read from a stale threads.json looks authoritative. prepare_context: honest (error payload L378). analyze/graph_traverse/get_transcript/search_transcripts/search_docs: honest.
- index_docs (src/tools/index-docs.ts + src/services/doc-index.ts): write path throws (writeLocalIndex L102) → server error; counts honest at call time. Durability gap: doc-index.ts:95 strips embeddings before writing docs-index.json; initDocVectorIndex L435-438 rebuilds from that file → 0 vector entries after restart → searchDocs L257 silently falls to BM25; index_docs then reports "up to date — no changes detected" (L139). "Chunks embedded: N" (L193) is true only for the current process lifetime.
- health (src/server.ts:367-383): honest; note write-health.ts:223 `skipped` → ok:true with summary "durability UNVERIFIED" → line reads "(ok, ...)".
- Confirmations: session-state.ts:537-541 addObservations returns 0 with no session (no error); session-columns.ts:74/92/114 drops task_observations on stores lacking it; schema/setup.sql has no task_observations/transcript_chunks/archived_at (grep) — gitmem_scar_usage IS provisioned (L132). enforcement.ts:116-118 reads confirmations from in-memory session, not the file.

---

### FINAL REPORT

Legend — gap: none / partial (claim over-states or hides a failure but the core write is verified) / false-success (a success claim with no evidence, or with contrary evidence). Severity: high / medium / low. "display" = the only text the agent sees (server.ts:429-430).

| # | tool / function | file:line | claim (field or display text) | what evidence exists at that point | gap | on a customer store: what the user is told vs what happened | reachable in normal use? | severity |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | session_close → syncThreadsToSupabase | src/tools/session-close.ts:1423,1443-1450,1687,1732; src/services/thread-supabase.ts:663-691,215-218,267-270,561-564 | display `STATUS.complete` header + "N threads"; result `success:true`; local threads.json pruned; session dir + closing-payload.json deleted | `all_synced` is `failed.length===0`, but the loop pushes every id to `synced` (L691) without reading createThreadInSupabase (returns null on failure), resolveThreadInSupabase (returns false) or touchThreadsInSupabase (swallows). Only a lookup throw (L657) records a failure. | false-success | Told: "close complete", threads synced. Happened: any/all thread creates/resolves rejected by the store; resolved threads dropped from threads.json; retry state deleted. The GIT-69 comment (L1401-1408) says this was fixed; the gate is hollow for write failures. | Yes — any RLS/constraint/network failure on gitmem_threads | high |
| 2 | session_close → recordScarUsageBatch | src/tools/session-close.ts:1606-1611,378-379,419-425,440,1727 | display "N scars applied" + one `STATUS.pass` line per scar; close_compliance.scars_applied | None. Batch is `getEffectTracker().track(...)` fire-and-forget; display rendered from `params.scars_to_record` before the promise settles; formatWriteWarnings (L440) reads the tracker before the batch reports. | false-success | Told: "✓ 3 scars applied" with check marks. Happened: batch may have inserted 0 rows (network, RLS, unresolved identifiers). Nothing later corrects it (see #3). | Yes — every close with surfaced scars | high |
| 3 | record_scar_usage_batch | src/tools/record-scar-usage-batch.ts:198-205 (+ effect-tracker.ts:103-113) | `success:true, durable:true, stored_in:"supabase"` (JSON, no display) | Reached whenever no upsert threw — including `resolvedCount===0`, `usage_ids:[]`, all entries in `failed_identifiers`. Tracker's reportedFailure only inspects `success===false`, so `health` counts it as success. | false-success | Told (by session_close's tracker): success. Happened: zero usage rows written because titles/IDs did not resolve (title matching L54-83 is exact/`includes` on an unordered 100-row page). | Yes — auto-bridge passes scar UUIDs (fine) but Q6 text and coerced `{title}` entries (session-close.ts:1039-1046) pass titles | medium |
| 4 | archive_learning (pro) | src/tools/archive-learning.ts:47-51,133-136,173,176 | display "Archived learning <id>.\n(Nms)"; `success:true, durable:true, stored_in:"supabase"` via `writeResult(hasSupabase())` | `await directPatch(...)` resolved — but its row array is discarded, and directPatch returns `[]` for zero matched rows (supabase-client.ts:510). A full-UUID input skips the existence lookup entirely (L49-51). | false-success | Told: archived, durable. Happened: no row matched (nonexistent UUID, wrong project prefix, RLS-hidden row) — nothing changed; cache flushed anyway. | Yes — any mistyped/foreign UUID | high |
| 5 | session_start session row | src/tools/session-start.ts:1149-1152,1300; createSessionRecord L464-473 | display `productLine("active")` + `<session_id> · <agent> · <project>` | None — the sessions upsert is in flight; `stored:false` is only consumed by metrics' FK guard (metrics.ts:170-178). | partial (confirmation-before-completion) | Told: session active with an ID. Happened: no durable row; the ID exists only in local files until session_close upserts it (which will then fail honestly if the store is still down). | Yes — store unreachable / sessions table missing | medium |

### The four GIT-101-excluded tools

**absorb_observations** (src/tools/absorb-observations.ts). Writes: (1) in-memory `addObservations` (session-state.ts:537-553) — verified by return count; (2) a Supabase upsert of `{id: sessionId, task_observations: getObservations()}` to gitmem_sessions (L71-80), gated on `supportedColumns(sessionsTable, ["task_observations"])`, launched as a bare promise with `.catch(console.error)` — not awaited, not effect-tracked, so it reaches neither the result nor `health`. The display is "Absorbed N observations (M scar candidates)" (L101) with N from the in-memory add. Outcome of the durable write never reaches result or display. Is that a gap? Yes, and it is a false-success rather than merely partial on customer stores: schema/setup.sql does not define task_observations, so the probe returns "missing" and nothing is written mid-session; at session_close the same column is stripped by filterToStoreSessionColumns (session-columns.ts:74,92,114). The tool description says it is called "to persist and analyze" observations, the display says absorbed, and on every customer store the observations are discarded at both points with no message. On nTEG's store (column present) the write is real but its failure is invisible. A secondary gap: with no active session the display says "Absorbed 0 observations" (L60→0) rather than refusing.

**dismiss_suggestion** (src/tools/dismiss-suggestion.ts). Writes: mutates the in-memory suggestions array (`dismissSuggestionById`, thread-suggestions.ts:157-168) and calls `saveSuggestions(suggestions)` (L66) which writes .gitmem/suggested-threads.json. `saveSuggestions` is `void` and swallows every write error (thread-suggestions.ts:206-220). The tool then returns `success:true` and display "Dismissed suggestion: <id>" (L69-74). The write's outcome cannot reach the result because the function discards it. Gap: false-success on I/O failure — the suggestion remains "pending" on disk and is re-offered at the next session_start while the tool said dismissed. Reachability is low (a local file write failing), which is why it is rated low; it is still a claim with no evidence. Note the tool is local-only on every tier, so "durable" semantics are the local file's.

**contribute_feedback** (src/tools/contribute-feedback.ts). Writes: (1) local `.gitmem/feedback/<date>-<type>-<id>.json` via `fs.writeFileSync` (L105) — throws on failure, so "Feedback recorded: <id> … Saved to .gitmem/feedback/<file>" (L144) is backed by evidence; (2) when `feedback_enabled`, a POST to the hard-coded community_feedback endpoint (feedback-remote.ts:26-43, throws on non-2xx) run under `getEffectTracker().track("feedback", ...)` (L118-137). `remoteSubmitted = true` is assigned unconditionally at L138 right after launching, so `remote_submitted:true` (L150) is a constant, not an outcome. The display says "(queued for remote)" (L143), which is accurate wording; the result field is not. Gap: partial — the display hedges correctly, but the result field over-states and the remote failure is visible only in `health`. Minor: the per-session quota is decremented (L69) before the local write, so a thrown write consumes a slot.

**cleanup_threads** (src/tools/cleanup-threads.ts). Writes: only when `auto_archive:true` on pro/dev — `archiveDormantThreads(project)` (L201) PATCHes `status:"archived"` per eligible row (thread-supabase.ts:755-758). Everything else is a read. `archiveDormantThreads` catches every error and returns `{archived_count:0, archived_ids:[]}` (L767-770); cleanup_threads copies those into the result (L202-203, L306-307) and display "Archived: N" (L116) with `success:true` (L303). So the outcome reaches the display only when nothing failed; a failed query, a failed first PATCH, or a failed Nth PATCH after N-1 successes all render as "Archived: 0". Gap: false-success (a store failure reported as a clean zero) and an under-report (partial archivals lost). Also, on free tier `auto_archive` is silently ignored (L129-194) and still prints "Archived: 0". The Step-2 read (L207) is not caught, so a failure THERE surfaces as a server error — honest, and inconsistent with the archive step.

### Counts

- Rows audited: 33 findings + 7 explicitly-honest groups (covering all 31 tools requested plus the write services).
- false-success: 7 — #1 session_close thread gate, #2 session_close scar usage, #3 record_scar_usage_batch, #4 archive_learning, #9 cleanup_threads archive, #11 dismiss_suggestion, #15 absorb_observations (customer stores).
- partial: 20 — #5, #6, #7, #8, #10, #12, #13, #14, #16, #17, #18, #20, #21, #23, #24, #25, #26, #27, #28, #30, #33 (of which read-side "error rendered as empty": #6, #24, #25, #26).
- mismatch / under-report only (no false success): 6 — #19, #22, #29, #31, #32, and the free-tier stored_in contradiction in #28.
- Honest: create_thread, resolve_thread (primary path), create_learning, create_decision, record_scar_usage, save_transcript, recall, prepare_context, analyze, graph_traverse, get_transcript, search_transcripts, search_docs, index_docs (write path), health, session_close session row, sessionCloseFree.
- Category tally: (1) success without evidence — #1,#2,#3,#4,#9,#11,#15; (2) constant/derived-from-input fields — #13 remote_submitted, #21 learnings/scars counts, #28 success:true on free, #31 ok:true skipped; (3) display vs result mismatch — #19,#22,#31; (4) confirmation-before-completion — #2,#5,#13,#15,#23; (5) excluded tools — see paragraphs; (6) session_start/refresh/confirm/reflect/index_docs/save_transcript/feedback/recall — #5-#8,#13,#20,#27,#30, recall honest.

### Top 10 (by user impact on a customer store)

1. #1 session_close thread-sync gate is hollow — `all_synced:true` while every thread write failed; local resolved threads pruned, retry state deleted, display "complete". (session-close.ts:1443-1450,1687,1732; thread-supabase.ts:663-691)
2. #2 session_close renders "N scars applied ✓" from its inputs before the fire-and-forget batch runs. (session-close.ts:1606-1611,378-425,1727)
3. #4 archive_learning reports durable success for a UUID that matched no row (PATCH result discarded; full UUID skips lookup). (archive-learning.ts:47-51,133,176)
4. #3 record_scar_usage_batch returns success:true/durable:true with zero rows written when identifiers fail to resolve; effect-tracker counts it as success. (record-scar-usage-batch.ts:198-205; effect-tracker.ts:103-113)
5. #15 absorb_observations says "Absorbed N" and its description says "persist"; on every setup.sql store observations are dropped mid-session (column probe) and again at close (column filter), silently. (absorb-observations.ts:71-80,101; session-columns.ts:74,92,114)
6. #9 cleanup_threads reports "Archived: 0" for store failures and for partial archivals. (cleanup-threads.ts:200-204,116; thread-supabase.ts:767-770)
7. #6 session_start shows "No threads or decisions." and the first-session welcome banner when Supabase is unreachable. (session-start.ts:280-283,1031-1034,1299)
8. #5 session_start prints "active · <id>" before the session row write resolves; a failed row is never reported. (session-start.ts:1149-1152,1300)
9. #24/#25 search and log render any query error as "No results found." / "No entries found." (search.ts:218-230,350-364; log.ts:191-200,283-294)
10. #27 index_docs claims "Chunks embedded: N" but embeddings are never persisted; after restart semantic doc search is silently BM25 and re-index says "up to date". (doc-index.ts:95,435-438; index-docs.ts:139,193)

## 5. CLASS C — Schema assumptions audit (gitmem v1.11.0, f93c3a3, branch audit/GIT-113-phase-0)


Read-only. Sources: `src/**` (116 .ts), `schema/setup.sql` (665 lines), `tests/fixtures/schema/setup-v1.8.0.sql` (657 lines), `tests/helpers/schema-drift.ts`, `tests/unit/schema-drift.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/blank-supabase.mjs`.
Table names below are resolved with the default prefix (`src/services/tier.ts:160-171`: `GITMEM_TABLE_PREFIX || "gitmem_"`).
Excluded (already reported by the GIT-109 analyzer, `tests/unit/schema-drift.test.ts:44-58`): the 3 probe-guarded columns (`gitmem_learnings.archived_at`, `gitmem_sessions.claude_code_session_id`, `gitmem_sessions.task_observations`) and the 8 dev-tier variant items (`variant_assignments`, `variant_performance_metrics`, `scar_enforcement_variants.{active,description,enforcement_config,...}`).

### v1.8.0 -> current setup.sql diff (complete)

`diff tests/fixtures/schema/setup-v1.8.0.sql schema/setup.sql` produces exactly two hunks:

1. `refresh_scar_behavioral_scores()` body — current adds a predicate (`schema/setup.sql:407-412`):
   `AND l.decay_multiplier IS DISTINCT FROM GREATEST(0.1, 1.0 - (us.times_dismissed::FLOAT / us.times_surfaced::FLOAT) * 0.8)` so unchanged rows are no longer rewritten (and no longer get `updated_at = NOW()`). Comment cites GIT-98.
2. `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS "Service role full access"` added before the two `CREATE POLICY` statements on `gitmem_licenses` / `gitmem_license_activations` (`schema/setup.sql:537-544`). Re-applicability only; nTEG-infra tables.

No table, column, view, index, RLS, trigger or RPC signature differs between the two versions. Every object listed below as "in current" is therefore also "in v1.8.0" unless stated.

Objects defined by setup.sql (both versions): tables `gitmem_learnings`, `gitmem_sessions`, `gitmem_decisions`, `gitmem_scar_usage`, `gitmem_threads`, `knowledge_triples`, `gitmem_query_metrics`, `scar_enforcement_variants`, `gitmem_licenses`, `gitmem_license_activations`; views `gitmem_learnings_lite`, `gitmem_sessions_lite`, `gitmem_decisions_lite`, `gitmem_threads_lite`; functions `gitmem_semantic_search`, `gitmem_scar_search`, `refresh_scar_behavioral_scores`, `gitmem_update_timestamp` (trigger fn), `gitmem_validate_license`, `gitmem_deactivate_device`; triggers on `gitmem_learnings` and `gitmem_sessions` only; **no storage buckets; no `match_*` functions; no `transcript_chunks`; no `variant_*` tables.**

Tables exist; this is a load/latency observation, not a missing-object one.

When the RPC path is taken on a pro store: `isLocalSearchReady()` is `initialized && scars.length > 0` (local-vector-search.ts:203-205, 381-384). It is false (a) from server start until the bulk load finishes, (b) permanently on a store whose indexed learnings set is empty (a fresh store — the first `recall` of a new customer), (c) after a failed bulk load, (d) whenever `GITMEM_SEARCH_MODE=remote` (config.ts:8,71-83; startup.ts:290-299). `scarSearch` calls `embed()` first (supabase-client.ts:673-678) so the 404 only appears when an embedding provider is configured — which is the normal pro configuration. The blank-store e2e (tests/e2e/blank-supabase.mjs) runs `recall` at line 347 against an empty store with a fake embedding server (lines 10-11, 162-214) and fails the run on any `/rest/v1/` 4xx not on `EXPECTED_FAILURES` (line 253; the list at line 81 is empty) — this path is expected to be visible there; not executed in this audit.

No `is_active` filter (returns archived learnings). | Identical. Never called by src/. |
| `gitmem_scar_search(query_embedding, match_count INT=5, similarity_threshold FLOAT=0.0)` setup.sql:322-376 | `gitmem_learnings` (id, title, description, severity, scar_type, counter_arguments, decay_multiplier, embedding, is_active, created_at) — hard-coded | Yes. Declares `decay_days`, `temporal_weight` (setup.sql:341-342) and never uses them (harmless). Temporal decay keyed on `scar_type IN ('process','incident','context')`; the column default is `'operational'` (setup.sql:17) and the code default is `'process'` (create-learning.ts:161) — both fall into "no decay" branches, so no error, but the two defaults disagree. | Identical. Never called by src/ (T2 HIGH). |
| `refresh_scar_behavioral_scores()` setup.sql:383-422 | `gitmem_scar_usage` (scar_id, reference_type, surfaced_at), `gitmem_learnings` (id, decay_multiplier, updated_at, is_active) — hard-coded | Yes. `reference_type IN ('none','refuted')` matches the values code writes (session-close.ts:739-746, 858, 875; record-scar-usage.ts:53). | **v1.8.0 body has no `IS DISTINCT FROM` guard** (current :407-412). On a v1.8.0 store every `session_start` (session-start.ts:1169-1170 -> behavioral-decay.ts:39-58) runs `UPDATE gitmem_learnings SET decay_multiplier = ..., updated_at = NOW()` for every learning with >=3 surfacings in 90 days, even when the multiplier is unchanged. `updated_at` is the GIT-98 cache fingerprint (supabase-client.ts:609-617 `order=updated_at.desc&limit=1`, startup.ts:160-172), so the on-disk vector cache is invalidated on every start and the full embedding download (`loadScarsWithEmbeddings`, ~20 KB/row per supabase-client.ts:566) re-runs each session. This is exactly the defect GIT-98 fixed — but the fix lives in a SQL body customers never re-apply. Also changes the meaning of the returned `scars_updated` (rows touched vs rows changed). |
| `gitmem_update_timestamp()` + triggers setup.sql:486-502 | `NEW.updated_at`; triggers on `gitmem_learnings`, `gitmem_sessions` only | Yes for those two. `gitmem_threads` (setup.sql:184) and `knowledge_triples` (:212) have `updated_at` columns with **no trigger**; `gitmem_decisions`, `gitmem_scar_usage`, `gitmem_query_metrics` have no `updated_at`. Triple writes set it explicitly (triple-writer.ts:324); thread PATCHes do not. archive-learning.ts:130 relies on the learnings trigger (holds). | Identical. |
| Views setup.sql:258-282 | column lists match their base tables | Yes. `gitmem_learnings_lite` omits `why_this_matters`, `action_protocol`, `self_check_criteria`, `repeat_mistake`, `related_scar_id`, `repeat_mistake_details`, `embedding`; `gitmem_sessions_lite` omits `recording_path`, `transcript_path`, `embedding`; `gitmem_threads_lite` omits `embedding` only. No `security_invoker` — views run with owner privileges (RLS on base tables bypassed through the view; only `service_role` is used by the client, so no code impact). | Identical. |
| RLS setup.sql:429-481 | 8 tables, two PERMISSIVE policies each | Policies are OR'd. `"Block anonymous access" ... USING (auth.role() != 'anon')` **grants** full access to every non-anon role (e.g. `authenticated`) rather than blocking anything the first policy did not already allow. Code is unaffected (uses `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY || SUPABASE_KEY`, supabase-client.ts:121-122; activate.ts:352-356 prompts for the service-role key). Schema fact, not a code drift. | Identical. |
| `gitmem_validate_license` / `gitmem_deactivate_device` setup.sql:557-665 | `gitmem_licenses`, `gitmem_license_activations`; `SECURITY DEFINER` | Yes. | Only the `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` lines around them differ (:538, :542). These run on every customer store despite setup.sql:505-507. |
| Indexes | `idx_gitmem_learnings_embedding` ivfflat `lists=10` built at setup time on an empty table (setup.sql:55-57) | Valid; index quality is fixed at creation. | Identical. |

Mechanism: `src/services/store-columns.ts:32-39` issues `GET /rest/v1/<table>?select=<cols>&limit=1`; 42703/42P01/PGRST204/PGRST205/"does not exist"/"Could not find the" => "missing"; any other error => "unknown" (not cached, treated as absent for that call). Answers cached per process (`known` map, :24). `storeHasTable(t)` = `supportedColumns(t, ["id"]).has("id")` (:66-68).

| probe | site | what it guards |
|---|---|---|
| `gitmem_learnings.archived_at` | archive-learning.ts:132 | PATCH payload key (known finding) |
| `gitmem_sessions.task_observations` | absorb-observations.ts:73 | upsert (known finding) |
| `gitmem_sessions.{blocked_by, children, claude_code_session_id, compacted, compacted_at, compacted_summary, handover_linear_slug, insights, metrics, pre_compaction_summary, task_observations}` — only those keys present in the payload | session-columns.ts:104-119 via session-close.ts:1548 | the session-close upsert (known) |
| table `gitmem_transcript_chunks` | session-close.ts:1479 | transcript upload + chunking on close (also stands in for the bucket, T3) |
| tables `gitmem_learnings`, `gitmem_decisions` (select id, limit 1) | write-health.ts:180-190 (startup) | write-path health message only |
| tables `gitmem_learnings`, `gitmem_sessions`, `gitmem_decisions`, `gitmem_scar_usage` (`select=id&limit=0`) | activate.ts:104-126 | `gitmem-mcp activate` schema check — **does not probe** `gitmem_threads`, `knowledge_triples`, `gitmem_query_metrics`, `scar_enforcement_variants`, the 4 views, or any function; a partially-applied setup.sql passes activation |
| `gitmem_learnings` `select=updated_at ... Prefer: count=exact` | supabase-client.ts:609-641 | vector-cache fingerprint (not a schema probe; consumes `updated_at` semantics — see T5 HIGH) |

nTEG-only objects reached with **no** probe and **no** tier gate:
- RPC `match_gitmem_learnings_weighted` (supabase-client.ts:693) — pro path, T2 HIGH.
- RPC `match_gitmem_learnings` (supabase-client.ts:190) — dead code.

### T8 — Findings, ranked

| # | severity | reach | finding | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **HIGH** | every Pro store, every time the local vector index is not ready (server start window, empty store / first recall of a new customer, failed bulk load, `GITMEM_SEARCH_MODE=remote`) | `scarSearch` calls RPC `match_gitmem_learnings_weighted`, which neither setup.sql version defines; setup.sql provisions `gitmem_scar_search` with a different name, parameter (`similarity_threshold` vs `match_threshold`) and a narrower return set. `recall`, `search`, `prepare_context` therefore error with PGRST202 on that path. The GIT-93 rename (CHANGELOG.md:219-223) targeted the function name found on nTEG's deployment, not the one setup.sql ships. Not probe-guarded. | supabase-client.ts:680-712; setup.sql:322-337 (both versions); recall.ts:543-565; search.ts:257-280; prepare-context.ts:359; local-vector-search.ts:203-205,381-384; config.ts:71-83; startup.ts:290-299 |
| 2 | **HIGH** | every v1.8.0 store with any learning surfaced >=3 times in 90 days (i.e. every active customer after a few sessions) | v1.8.0 `refresh_scar_behavioral_scores` rewrites `updated_at = NOW()` on unchanged rows at every `session_start`; `updated_at` is the GIT-98 disk-cache fingerprint, so the on-disk embedding cache is invalidated and the full learnings download re-runs on every start. The fix exists only in the current setup.sql body, which customers never re-apply. The package cannot detect which body a store runs. | setup.sql:402-412 vs setup-v1.8.0.sql:402-407; session-start.ts:1169-1170; behavioral-decay.ts:39-58; supabase-client.ts:598-641; startup.ts:156-172 |
| 3 | MED-HIGH | every Pro store; every `archive_learning` call with a short id prefix | `id=like.<prefix>%` against a `UUID` primary key — Postgres has no `uuid ~~ text` operator; expected 42883/400 on every prefix archive (full-UUID input bypasses the query). Tests mock the query. | archive-learning.ts:53-66; setup.sql:12; tests/unit/tools/archive-learning.test.ts:122,144 |
| 4 | MED | every Pro store; every `create_learning` that passes `action_protocol` or `self_check_criteria` | JSON arrays written to `TEXT` columns. migrate-local.ts:112-120 records that this produced PostgREST 400s and coerces on migration only; `create_learning` sends the raw array (`hasEnforcementFields()` exists but is unused). Not verified live. | create-learning.ts:119-121; schemas/create-learning.ts:32-33; types/index.ts:342-343; setup.sql:30-31; recall.ts:117-118; tier.ts:153 |

Only `gitmem_query_metrics` has the GIT-73 guard. | create-decision.ts:54,87; record-scar-usage.ts:41,48,65; record-scar-usage-batch.ts:132,144,169; session-start.ts:440-473,1151; metrics.ts:139-178; setup.sql:114,135 |

Silent. | recall.ts:114,200-201,660; supabase-client.ts:530-531; setup.sql:11-39 |
| 9 | LOW | nTEG (non-default prefix) and diagnostics | Literal table names bypass `GITMEM_TABLE_PREFIX`: `gitmem_query_metrics` (all 4 metrics sites), `gitmem_learnings`/`gitmem_sessions`/`gitmem_decisions`/`gitmem_scar_usage` in check/activate. Under `orchestra_` metrics still write to `gitmem_query_metrics`. | metrics.ts:219,430,446,467; check.ts:145,354,452; activate.ts:105 |

| 11 | LOW | every customer store | setup.sql provisions objects the client never uses (`gitmem_semantic_search`, `gitmem_scar_search`, `gitmem_licenses`, `gitmem_license_activations`, `gitmem_validate_license`, `gitmem_deactivate_device` — the last four despite setup.sql:505-507 saying users need not run them) and omits objects the client does use (`match_*`, bucket, `transcript_chunks`). | setup.sql:287-376, 505-665 |

No effect on the client (service-role key), but a schema fact both versions share. | setup.sql:440-481 |
| 13 | LOW | `gitmem-mcp activate` | Schema check probes 4 of 8 tables and no views/functions; a partial setup.sql passes. | activate.ts:104-126 |

Not findings (verified consistent): every CHECK-constrained value the code writes (`learning_type`, `severity`, `reference_type`, thread `status`/`thread_class`, triple `predicate`) is in the corresponding list; `source_id` UUID is guarded (GIT-105); embeddings are sent as `"[...]"` strings which pgvector accepts; `metadata->>session_id` targets a key the code itself writes; migrate-local `KNOWN_COLUMNS` match setup.sql exactly; the `_lite` views contain every column the code selects from them; `ThreadRow`, `analytics.SessionRecord`, `RawTriple`, `KnowledgeTriple`, `LogEntry`, `DecisionRecord` map to existing columns.

## 6. Class D — Environment Assumptions (gitmem v1.11.0, f93c3a3, branch audit/GIT-113-phase-0)


Read-only sweep of `src/`, `bin/`, `hooks/scripts/`, `scripts/` (tests excluded). Line numbers are exact for f93c3a3.
Empirical checks were run on the audit host (macOS 14.6 / Darwin 23.6, bash 3.2.57, Node 22.17, no `jq`, no `timeout`/`gtimeout`, `pgrep` present, hostname `Chriss-MacBook-Pro-4.local`, `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=claude-desktop`, `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` unset in the tool shell, `~/.gitmem` registry holding 2 live sessions from 2 distinct npx-launched server pids). Where a claim rests on such a check it is marked **[verified]**; everything else is from reading the code.

Severity scale: **Critical** = silently disables or corrupts a shipped feature in a normal customer setup; **High** = wrong behaviour reachable in a common setup; **Medium** = wrong behaviour in a plausible but less common setup; **Low** = cosmetic / theoretical / dev-only.

---

### Category 1 — cwd-relative paths

| # | file:line | code (short quote) | assumption | breaks when / on which platform | reachable in normal use? | severity |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | bin/init-wizard.js:25,169,397,440,452,498 | `const cwd = process.cwd(); const gitmemDir = join(cwd, ".gitmem")` | The store the wizard seeds (`learnings.json`, `threads.json`, `config.json`, `closing-payload.json`) is the store the server reads | Since GIT-91 (1.8.0) the server reads `~/.gitmem` (or `GITMEM_DIR`); the wizard writes no `GITMEM_DIR` into the MCP config it generates (buildMcpConfig, :253-268). `npx gitmem-mcp init` therefore seeds `<project>/.gitmem/` which the server never reads, and because it writes `config.json` there, `isLiveGitmemRoot` (gitmem-dir.ts:287) flags it as stranded on every `session_start` | Yes — every fresh CLI-driven install | **Critical** |
| 1.2 | bin/gitmem.js:122-130, 224-236 | `cmdInit`: `join(process.cwd(), ".gitmem")` + writes `config.json` (`project`) | Same as 1.1 for `gitmem init --project X` on the legacy command | `--project` is written to a `config.json` the server never consults (server reads `getConfigProject()` from `~/.gitmem/config.json`, gitmem-dir.ts:355) | Yes | **High** |
| 1.3 | bin/gitmem.js:532-546, bin/init-wizard.js:272,342,373 | hooks copied to `<cwd>/.gitmem/hooks`, hook commands written as relative `bash .gitmem/hooks/x.sh` | Claude Code / Cursor runs hooks with cwd = project root | Holds for Claude Code (hooks run in project dir). Creates a project-scoped `.gitmem/` dir containing only `hooks/` — not "live", so no warning, but it is the same directory 1.1 writes to, so the two collide | Yes, benign alone | Low |
| 1.4 | hooks/scripts/auto-retrieve-hook.sh:145-159 | `SCRIPT_DIR/../../dist/hooks/quick-retrieve.js`, then `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/../dist/...`, then `/workspace/gitmem/dist/...`, `$(npm root -g)/gitmem/dist/...`, `.../lib/node_modules/gitmem/dist/...` | Built JS sits two levels above the scripts dir; global package is named `gitmem` | (a) In the copied-hooks layout (1.3) `SCRIPT_DIR=<project>/.gitmem/hooks` → `../../dist` = `<project>/dist` — exists only in the gitmem repo itself. (b) Package name is `gitmem-mcp` (package.json:2), so both global candidates look in a directory that never exists. (c) npx installs live in `~/.npm/_npx/<hash>/node_modules/gitmem-mcp/` — not a candidate. (d) `/workspace/gitmem` is an nTEG container path. Net: for any customer not using `node_modules/gitmem-mcp/hooks/scripts` directly, quick-retrieve is never found and the hook exits 0 silently (:167-170) | Yes — every `init`-installed customer | **Critical** (compounded by 6.2) |
| 1.5 | hooks/scripts/session-start.sh:195-196 | `[ -f ".gitmem/config.json" ] && jq -r '.project' .gitmem/config.json` | The project-scoped `.gitmem/config.json` in cwd names the session's project | Reads the pre-1.8.0 project-scoped config (or the one 1.1/1.2 just wrote) while the server's own default comes from `~/.gitmem/config.json`. Three project sources disagree (hook cwd config, server home config, `GITMEM_DEFAULT_PROJECT` server.ts:522). Also silently skipped without `jq` (no node fallback here, unlike every other JSON read in the hooks) | Yes | Medium |
| 1.6 | hooks/scripts/session-start.sh:65 | `for MCP_FILE in ".mcp.json" ".claude/mcp.json"` | cwd = project root | Intended; holds under Claude Code | Yes | Low |
| 1.7 | hooks/scripts/resolve-root.sh:37-40 | relative `GITMEM_DIR` → `$PWD/$GITMEM_DIR` | Hook cwd == server cwd | Server does **not** resolve a relative `GITMEM_DIR` at all (gitmem-dir.ts:59-65 returns `envDir` verbatim; every later `path.join` resolves it against the server's cwd at call time). A relative or `~/…` `GITMEM_DIR` yields two different stores (and `~` is expanded by neither) | Only if user sets a relative/`~` `GITMEM_DIR` | Medium |
| 1.8 | src/services/gitmem-dir.ts:223 | `let dir = process.cwd()` (findStrandedProjectRoots) | The MCP server's cwd is the project tree | Under the desktop app / user-scoped `~/.claude.json` servers the server cwd is not the repo; stranded detection then never sees the project's `.gitmem`, so the R18 notice (session-start.ts:966-987) is cwd-dependent while resolution is not | Yes (desktop) | Medium |
| 1.9 | src/commands/migrate-root.ts:163-165, 305 | `findProjectRoots() = findStrandedProjectRoots()` | CLI is run from inside the project | Documented ("above the current directory", :308); fine for a CLI | Yes | Low |
| 1.10 | src/tools/session-close.ts:120-122, 668-670, 135-149 | `const cwd = process.cwd(); … cwdFull.replace(/\//g, "-")` → `~/.claude/projects/<encoded>` | Server cwd == the cwd Claude Code encoded for the transcript dir | (a) Desktop app / user-scoped server: cwd is not the project → no match → no transcript. (b) Symlinked or `/private/var` vs `/var` cwd: the encoded string differs from Claude Code's own encoding (GIT-107 class, string-only, no realpath). (c) Multi-chat on one project: picks the most recently modified `.jsonl` regardless of which chat is closing (:181-186) — wrong transcript attached / wrong `claude_session_id` extracted | Yes (pro transcript metadata; dev transcript upload) | Medium |
| 1.11 | src/commands/activate.ts:541-548 | `projectRoot = process.cwd()` → append `.gitmem/` to `<cwd>/.gitignore` | Store is project-scoped | Harmless (only a gitignore entry); leftover from pre-1.8.0 | Yes | Low |
| 1.12 | src/commands/telemetry.ts:19,27 | `join(process.cwd(), "package.json")`, `join(process.cwd(), ".gitmem")` | cwd is the gitmem package AND the store | Version reads as `0.0.0` from any other cwd; telemetry state file goes to `<cwd>/.gitmem`, not the server's root; bypasses `getGitmemDir()` entirely | `gitmem telemetry` subcommand only | Medium |
| 1.13 | src/hooks/quick-retrieve.ts:154 | `process.argv[1]?.endsWith("quick-retrieve.js")` | Invoked by absolute path | Fine | Yes | Low |
| 1.14 | bin/uninstall.js:23,65-126 | `cwd`-relative `.mcp.json`, `.claude/`, `.gitmem` | cwd = project | Same class as 1.1; uninstall preserves `<cwd>/.gitmem` (:375) which is not the store the server used | Yes | Low |
| 1.15 | bin/gitmem.js:32,87,110,495; bin/init-wizard.js:24,81-172; src/commands/activate.ts:166-167 | `__dirname` via `fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)` + `..`/`../..` | Package layout identical for global / npx / local | Holds: `bin/` and `dist/commands/` are both one/two levels below package root in all three layouts and `files` (package.json) ships `schema/`, `hooks/`, templates | Yes | Low |

| # | file:line | code (short quote) | assumption | breaks when / on which platform | reachable in normal use? | severity |

Verified on macOS **[verified]**: `fs.realpathSync.native` normalises both `/var→/private/var` and path case (`/USERS/chriscrawford → /Users/chriscrawford`), so `sameDirectory` is sound where it is used; the defect is that it is used only in stranded-root detection and `migrate-root`.

**Count: 8 real sites (0 High, 2 Medium, 6 Low) + 2 cross-references.**

### Category 3 — tier / licence assumptions

Tier detection (src/services/tier.ts:27-57): `GITMEM_TIER` env → licence key (env `GITMEM_API_KEY` or `~/.gitmem/config.json.api_key`) with 72 h cache → *optimistic* `pro` before validation → no key but `SUPABASE_URL` (env or config.json) → `pro` (or `dev` if `GITMEM_DEV`) → else `free`.

| # | file:line | flag / code | what it gates | assumption | breaks when | reachable in normal use? | severity |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

The GIT-93 comment (:680-691) says the survey was of *deployed* (nTEG) functions. On every customer store the fallback returns PGRST202 whenever the local vector index is cold (first recall after start, cache miss, flush) | Yes — pro, first recall after every server start | **High** |
| 3.2 | src/services/tier.ts:47-56 | `if (!supabaseUrl) return "free"; … return "pro"` | Entire pro feature set | "backward compat for us" | Any customer who sets `SUPABASE_URL`/`supabase_url` without a licence key runs pro. Not a runtime break, but the licence gate is advisory only | Yes | Low (design) |
| 3.3 | src/services/tier.ts:29-31 | `GITMEM_TIER=dev` accepted from env | Everything dev-only (variants, transcripts, batch tools) | Only nTEG sets it | Any user can set it; dev paths then hit tables absent from setup.sql: `variant_assignments`, `variant_performance_metrics` (recall.ts:607, variant-assignment.ts:101,140), `transcript_chunks` + RPC `match_transcript_chunks` (search-transcripts.ts:139), storage bucket `session-transcripts` (supabase-client.ts:804). Gated tools return errors; nothing corrupts | Only if user opts in | Low |
| 3.4 | src/services/tier.ts:42-44 + server.ts:496-502 | optimistic `"pro"` then async `setTier("free")` on validation failure | Tier at startup | Downgrade re-evaluates lazily everywhere | Singletons built before validation finishes are **not** reset: `storage.ts:137 _storage` (built as `SupabaseStorage` if any tool ran early), `config.ts:135 configInstance`, `startup.ts:42-44` background init already started with `hasSupabase()` true (server.ts:518-523). `resetStorage()`/`reloadConfig()` are never called in production (grep: only tests). After downgrade, `getStorage()` callers (record-scar-usage.ts:74, create-decision.ts:113, create-learning.ts:284, search.ts:142, log.ts:122, recall.ts:443, prepare-context.ts:288, agent-briefing.ts:25, session-start.ts:523, session-close.ts:227, archive-learning.ts:78/146) keep writing/reading Supabase while `writeResult(hasSupabase())` reports "local" | Race: a tool call within ~1 s of startup with an invalid/expired key | Medium |
| 3.5 | src/services/tier.ts:95-97, 100-102, 143-155 | `hasEmbeddings`, `hasCompliance`, `hasAdvancedAgentDetection`, `hasMultiProject`, `hasEnforcementFields` | nothing | – | **Zero callers outside tier.ts** **[verified by grep]** — dead flags; documentation implies gating that does not exist (e.g. "compliance validation dev only" — compliance runs for all tiers in session-close) | – | Low |
| 3.6 | src/services/tier.ts:137-140; src/tools/recall.ts:602 | `hasMetrics()` only used as `hasVariants() && hasMetrics()` | nothing on pro | – | `recordMetrics` (metrics.ts:187) gates on `hasSupabase()`, so metrics are written on pro regardless of `hasMetrics()`; the flag is effectively dead | – | Low |

Variant tables: `scar_enforcement_variants` is in setup.sql:245 but the other two are not (dev-only, gated by 3.3) | pro: OK; dev: nTEG only | Low |

**Count: 15 sites (0 Critical, 2 High, 2 Medium, 11 Low).** No case was found where `pro` unlocks a *write* to an nTEG-only object; the two real breaks are a *read* RPC that setup.sql never creates (3.1) and env-only credential reads that bypass `config.json` (3.9/3.11).

---

### Category 4 — hostname / PID as identity (exhaustive)

Identity model as implemented: a session is keyed by `(hostname, pid, project)` in the registry (GIT-86) and by `sessions/<id>/session.json` on disk (GIT-89); resume/adoption gates on `hostname` equality and on `process.kill(pid, 0)`.

Empirical context **[verified]**: audit host hostname is `Chriss-MacBook-Pro-4.local` — the `-4` suffix is macOS/Bonjour's automatic rename after a name conflict, i.e. this machine's hostname has already changed at least four times. The registry holds two sessions from two distinct server pids (44778, 44790) — one process per chat here, not one process for all.

| # | file:line | code (short quote) | assumption | breaks when / on which platform | reachable in normal use? | severity |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4.1 | src/services/active-sessions.ts:320 | `if (typeof data.hostname === "string" && data.hostname !== currentHostname) continue;` | `os.hostname()` is stable for the life of a session | **This is the gate on session resume.** macOS changes `os.hostname()` on network change (`.local`↔`.lan`, `-2/-3/-4` conflict suffixes, sleep/wake on a new Wi-Fi); Docker/WSL restarts can change it; DHCP-assigned Linux hosts too. After a change, every existing `session.json` is skipped: MCP restart recovery (session-state.ts:123) returns null → "No active session"; `session_start` (session-start.ts:754) opens a *new* session; the old one lingers 24 h (pruneStale :465) and, being in the registry, is treated as live by the hooks (see 4.19) | Yes (laptops) | **High** |

(b) Store on a shared volume between host and container (or two containers): host pids are invisible → `ESRCH` → every foreign session looks dead → **adopted by a process that is not its owner** (GIT-20 violation). `EPERM` on macOS confirmed for pid 1, `ESRCH` for 999999 **[verified]** | (b): Docker bind-mounting `~/.gitmem` | Medium |
