### Top 10 (class D)

1. **7.1 / 6.1 — auto-retrieve hook dead on stock macOS** (`timeout` missing, failure discarded). Critical, every Mac hook user.
2. **1.1 — `init` seeds `<cwd>/.gitmem`, the server reads `~/.gitmem`**: starter scars, config (project) and threads of a fresh install are never read; the next session_start calls the new store "NOT being read". Critical, every new install via the wizard.
3. **4.24 / 9.2 — hooks key state on `$$`** when `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` is unset: recall nag and Stop-hook meaningful-session detection never accumulate. High.
4. **9.1 — GITMEM_DIR in the MCP env block isn't seen by hooks**: server and hooks on different stores. High.
5. **4.1 / 7.2 — hostname drift makes sessions unresumable** and orphans registry slots on laptops. High.
6. **4.5, 4.15, 4.22** — pid-as-identity in the desktop app's one-process model (see D4 table). High.
7. **3.x — remote recall RPC absent from setup.sql** (also C-1); pro "unlocks" a read path customers can't serve. High.
8. **7.3 / 9.5 — second cache root under `$HOME/.cache` or `/tmp`**, ignoring GITMEM_DIR. Medium.
9. **6.3, 6.4, 6.7** — see category 6 table (tool-dependence mediums). Medium.
10. **9.3 — `check` reads credentials from env only.** Medium.

## 7. CLASS E — CONCURRENCY AUDIT (gitmem v1.11.0, f93c3a3, branch audit/GIT-113-phase-0)


Read-only audit. All line numbers are exact against the working tree at f93c3a3. Facts only; severity scale: CRITICAL / HIGH / MED / LOW.

**Execution-model facts that everything below rests on**

- The MCP SDK (`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.26.0) dispatches every incoming request with `Promise.resolve().then(() => handler(request, fullExtra))` (`node_modules/@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/dist/esm/shared/protocol.js:355-363`), invoked directly from the transport's `onmessage` (`:237`). Nothing serializes tool calls: two `tools/call` requests arriving on the same stdio transport run as two interleaving async handlers. `src/server.ts:127-469` registers one `CallToolRequestSchema` handler with no queue/mutex. So "tool calls are sequential" holds only when the client sends them one at a time (Claude Code CLI does; the desktop app serving several chats over one process does not).
- All local file I/O in `src/` is synchronous (`fs.*Sync`). Within one process a sync read-modify-write cannot interleave with another sync RMW; it *can* interleave with an async flow that read the same file before an `await`. Across processes there is no serialization except `withLockSync` (registry only) and the O_EXCL download lock (vector cache only).
- The `.gitmem` root is `~/.gitmem` for every process regardless of cwd (`src/services/gitmem-dir.ts:57-96`), so every CLI session, every Cursor window, the desktop app, every bash hook and every `bin/gitmem.js` command share one registry, one `threads.json`, one `closing-payload.json`, one set of free-tier collection files and one `cache/` directory.

---

### 2. File writes — atomic vs direct overwrite vs append

Legend: **A** = temp file + `rename` (reader sees old or new, never partial); **D** = direct `writeFileSync` (truncate-then-write; a crash or a concurrent reader mid-write yields a truncated file); **App** = `appendFileSync` / shell `>>`.

#### 2.1 Every write site

| # | file:line | file written | class | reader's behaviour on truncated/invalid JSON |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | `src/services/active-sessions.ts:33-48` `atomicWriteFileSync` (used by `writeRegistry` :124 and `persistSessionPid` :386) | `active-sessions.json`; `sessions/<id>/session.json` (pid rebind only) | **A** — but falls back to a **D** write on any rename error (:39-47) | `readRegistry` :95-113: parse/Zod failure → `{sessions: []}` "starting fresh" (:107-111) → next `registerSession` writes a registry containing only itself; every other live session's entry is dropped (consequence in 4(f)). |
| 2.2 | `src/services/vector-disk-cache.ts:242-243` | `cache/learnings-vectors-<key>.json` | **A** (tmp name includes pid + `Date.now()`) | `readCacheFile` :207-217 → miss with reason; `deltaSyncFromCache` returns null → full download. Safe. |
| 2.3 | `src/commands/migrate-root.ts:283-284` | `learnings.json`, `threads.json`, `decisions.json`, `sessions.json` (merged) | **A** | n/a |
| 2.4 | `src/commands/migrate-root.ts:287` | `migrate-root-conflicts.json` | **D** | `loadConflicts` :217-224 → `[]` → previously logged conflicts are re-logged (duplicate entries), nothing lost. |
| 2.5 | `src/tools/session-start.ts:834` (`writeSessionFiles`, whole-object replace), `:667`, `:1307`, `:1500` (display RMW) | `sessions/<id>/session.json` | **D** | `session-state.ts:130` recover → throws → catch :192 → `null` → "No active session"; `active-sessions.ts:309-311` → skipped as not resumable; `pruneStale` :450 sees the file exists → **not** pruned; hooks `recall-check.sh:81-83` `jq ... \|\| echo "0"` → both counts 0 → confirmation gate bypassed and nag armed. |
| 2.6 | `src/services/session-state.ts:280, 307, 334, 410` (`setRecallCalled`, `setRecallFailure`, `clearRecallFailure`, `persistSurfacedScars`) | `session.json` | **D** (RMW) | as 2.5; on parse failure each writer catches (:281, 308, 335, 411) and keeps state in memory only. |

#### 2.2 Counts

- Atomic temp+rename: **3 helpers** (`atomicWriteFileSync`, `writeVectorCache`, `migrate-root` merged files) covering 2 JSON stores (`active-sessions.json`, `learnings-vectors-*.json`) plus one-off migration output.
- Direct-overwrite writes of **JSON stores the server later parses**: `session.json` (11 sites), `threads.json` (1 helper, 4 callers), `suggested-threads.json` (1 helper, 3 callers), free-tier collections (1 helper, all free-tier writes), `docs-index.json`, `hook-scars.json`, `license-cache.json`, `config.json` (6 CLI sites), `~/.cache/gitmem/results/*`, `telemetry.json`, `migrate-root-conflicts.json` — **~30 sites, 11 distinct stores**.
- Readers that respond to a truncated store by **silently starting from empty and then overwriting it on the next write**: `local-file-storage.ts:44` (4 collections), `thread-manager.ts:257`, `thread-suggestions.ts:198`, `active-sessions.ts:107-111`, `doc-index.ts:79`. These are the loss amplifiers: a one-time partial read becomes a permanent overwrite.

---

### 3. Lock-free read→modify→write on JSON stores

`withLockSync` (`src/services/file-lock.ts:145`) is used at exactly five sites, all on `active-sessions.json`: `registerSession` (`active-sessions.ts:150`), `unregisterSession` (:182), `reconcileRegistryEntry` (:402), `pruneStale` (:441), `migrateFromLegacy` (:563). Every other RMW below runs unlocked.

| # | file:line (read → write) | store | interleaving processes / paths | what is lost | reachable? | severity |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3.A | `src/tools/session-start.ts:862` `loadThreadsFile()` → `:894` `saveThreadsFile(merged)`; `src/tools/create-thread.ts:319-321` load→push→save; `src/tools/resolve-thread.ts:70-73` (in-memory session array **or** file) → `:196` `saveThreadsFile(threads)`; `src/tools/session-close.ts:1450` `saveThreadsFile(openThreadsOnly)` where `closeThreads` derives from `getThreads()` + params (:581-590, :1414), **never from the file** | `threads.json` | (i) Two CLI windows: P1 `session_start` reads at t0; P2 `create_thread` reads+writes at t1; P1 saves its t0-based merge at t2 → P2's thread gone. (ii) `resolve_thread` in a session whose state has threads writes the **session array** back (:196), replacing every file entry the session never held — including threads created by another process since this session started. (iii) `session_close` prune (:1450) writes the close-time array from memory; anything another process added to the file after this session's `session_start` is dropped. (iv) Desktop: chat B's `session_start` `saveThreadsFile(merged)` while chat A's `create_thread` sits between its `await createThreadInSupabase` (:300) and its `loadThreadsFile()` (:319) — fine; but A's `resolve_thread` writes A-session array over B's merge. | Pro tier: cache loss only (Supabase is SOT; `list_threads` reads Supabase). Free tier: `threads.json` **is** the store; a thread that reaches only the file is lost unless its creating session closes and the thread lands in that session's `sessions.json` record (`aggregateThreads` re-derives from closed sessions ≤14 days, `thread-manager.ts:141-152`). | Yes — two CLI windows or CLI + desktop on the same machine (normal for this product) | MED (free tier HIGH) |
| 3.B | `src/services/session-state.ts:277→280, 305→307, 331→334, 408→410`; `src/tools/confirm-scars.ts:148→154`; `src/tools/reflect-scars.ts:130→132`; `src/tools/session-start.ts:664→667, 1305→1307, 1498→1500` (display), `:805-834` (`writeSessionFiles`: reads only `started_at`/`recall_called`, then **replaces the whole object**); `src/services/active-sessions.ts:384→386` `persistSessionPid` (unlocked, called at :368 before the lock at :369) | `sessions/<id>/session.json` | Per-session file, so cross-process interleaving needs two processes bound to one session: (i) adoption of the same dead-PID session by two restarting servers (4(b)); (ii) `bin/gitmem.js session-close --session_id` / any process passing an explicit `session_id` (session-close.ts:1331 does no ownership check). Within one process all writers are sync, so no interleaving — but the **wholesale rewrite** at `writeSessionFiles` :816-834 is itself lossy: `session_refresh` (:1444) rewrites `session.json` with `surfaced_scars`, `threads`, `started_at`, `recall_called` only → `confirmations` (confirm-scars.ts:149), `reflections` (reflect-scars.ts:131), `recall_failure`, `display`, `recording_path` are **dropped from disk**. `setCurrentSession` at :1456 also drops in-memory confirmations/reflections/observations/children. | After `session_refresh`: hook `recall-check.sh:97-99` reads `confirmations` = 0 with `surfaced_scars` > 0 → **hard block** on the next consequential Bash/Write/Edit (:147-166) for scars the agent already confirmed; in-memory `hasUnconfirmedScars()` (session-state.ts:520) also flips true → server-side enforcement banner. | Yes — `session_refresh` is the documented post-compaction step | MED |
| 3.C | `src/services/active-sessions.ts` all writers under lock; readers `listActiveSessions` :206, `findSessionByHostPid` :218, `findSessionById` :228 unlocked; hooks `resolve-root.sh:64-66` (`jq`) | `active-sessions.json` | Reads without lock are safe because writes are `rename`d (2.1). Only the rename-failure fallback (:46) exposes a partial file. | — | — | LOW |
| 3.D | `src/tools/session-close.ts:1586→1593` (inside the fire-and-forget embedding `track`, i.e. **after** the close has returned and after `cleanupSessionFiles`); `src/tools/promote-suggestion.ts:60→97` (with `await createThread` between); `src/tools/dismiss-suggestion.ts:53→66` | `suggested-threads.json` | Two sessions closing within seconds; or (desktop) a `dismiss_suggestion` while another chat's `promote_suggestion` awaits `createThread`. | A pending suggestion silently reverts or disappears; a promoted suggestion's `promoted_thread_id` link lost. | Occasional | LOW |
| 3.E | `src/services/local-file-storage.ts:132→141` `upsert` (read whole collection → replace/push → write whole collection); `:149→152` `delete`; `:217→231` `loadStarterScars`; callers: `session-start.ts:584` (sessions), `session-close.ts:289` (sessions), `:294-303` (scar_usage, one RMW **per scar**), free-tier `create_learning`/`create_decision`/`archive_learning` via `getStorage().upsert` | `learnings.json`, `sessions.json`, `decisions.json`, `scar_usage.json` | Two free-tier processes (two CLI windows, or CLI + hook `quick-retrieve` reads): P1 `session_start` upsert reads `sessions.json`; P2 `session_close` reads; P1 writes; P2 writes → P1's session row is gone → P1's later `session_close` `storage.get` returns `null` (session-close.ts:246) → record written as `{id, close_compliance, closing_reflection…}` **without** `session_date`/`agent`/`project` (:248-251) → `aggregateThreads` drops it (`s.session_date >= cutoffStr` with `undefined`, thread-manager.ts:151) → its open threads never resurface. Two `create_learning` calls in two windows: one learning lost outright. | Free tier: **permanent loss of a learning/decision/session/scar_usage record**; combined with 2.11 ("starting fresh") a single torn read wipes the collection. | Yes — free tier + more than one Claude Code window is the default install shape | HIGH |
| 3.F | `src/tools/session-close.ts:976` `getGitmemPath("closing-payload.json")` — **one path per `~/.gitmem`, not per session**; read :984-992, deleted :1695; hooks direct the agent to write exactly this path (`hooks/scripts/session-close-check.sh:97-101`, `resolve-root.sh:44`); `bin/init-wizard.js:454` pre-seeds it | `closing-payload.json` | Agent A writes payload; agent B (other window/chat) writes payload (overwrites); A calls `session_close(S_A)` → reads **B's reflection**, stores it on S_A, deletes the file; B's `session_close(S_B)` → `payloadConsumed=false`, no inline reflection → returns "closing-payload.json not found … write the closing payload" (:1005-1025); B rewrites, closes with its own — but S_A now permanently carries B's reflection, decisions, `scars_to_record`, `open_threads` (:987 merges file into params). Ordering variants: B's close first → A's reflection lost and A told to write it again. | Wrong session gets another session's closing reflection/decisions/threads; the other session's ceremony must be redone. | Yes — any two concurrent sessions on one machine that close within the same window | HIGH |
| 3.G | `src/services/license.ts:132` write; `:101-120` read | `license-cache.json` | Two servers validating at once; `deactivate` unlink (:145) during a server's read. | None material (readers null → revalidate). | Yes | LOW |
| 3.H | `src/commands/activate.ts:252→534` (reads `config.json` early, prompts interactively, writes back minutes later); `deactivate.ts:70→98`; `bin/gitmem.js:141→143, 231→234`; `bin/init-wizard.js:446-448` | `config.json` | A running server never writes `config.json`, so the only RMW race is CLI-vs-CLI (rare). But `activate` rewrites the whole object from a stale read taken before the wizard prompts. | Fields another tool added meanwhile are lost. | Rare | LOW |
| 3.I | `src/services/startup.ts:57-82` (write only); `src/services/vector-disk-cache.ts:115-139` (read only) | `cache/hook-scars.json`, `cache/learnings-vectors-*.json` | Vector cache: atomic, plus single-flight lock (4(e)). `hook-scars.json`: N servers × (startup + every 15 min) overwrite the same file; content is the same corpus, so only the torn-read window matters (2.13) — **except** the empty-overwrite path in 1.8. | Transient miss; permanent `[]` after a failed refresh in any one process. | Yes | MED (via 1.8) |
| 3.J | `src/services/doc-index.ts:149→219` (`await embed` per chunk between read and write) | `docs-index.json` | Two `index_docs` calls (desktop chats, or two windows). | Chunks from the slower call overwritten by the faster one's merge. | Rare | LOW |
| 3.K | `src/lib/telemetry.ts` `loadConfig` :70→`saveConfig` :112 | `telemetry.json` | CLI only. | — | — | LOW |

---

### 4. Two processes sharing `~/.gitmem` — scenario walkthroughs

#### 4(a) CLI session and desktop app both call `session_start`

Two processes P_cli, P_desk, distinct PIDs, same `~/.gitmem`.

1. Both run `checkExistingSession` (`src/tools/session-start.ts:732-773`): `migrateFromLegacy()` (:744, locked, idempotent via new-registry check `active-sessions.ts:569`), `pruneStale()` (:747, locked), `findSessionByHostPid(host, ownPid, project)` (:754) — misses for a fresh process, then `findResumableSessionOnDisk(project)` (`active-sessions.ts:286`): scans every `sessions/*/session.json`; the other process's session has a live foreign PID → `pidAlive: true` (:341) → never adopted (:359). Each gets `null` → new UUID.
2. `writeSessionFiles` (:779): `session.json` into its own dir (:834), then `registerSession` under the lock (:844); slots differ by PID so no displacement (`sameSlot` :137-139). Then the **unlocked** `threads.json` merge (:862-894): both read, both write; on pro both hold the Supabase set plus ≤24 h local-only threads (:873-877) so the last writer's file is a superset of what it read but may omit a thread the *other* process added between its read and write (3.A). On free tier both read the same file and write merged-with-file, so the only loss is a `create_thread` that lands between one process's read and write.
3. Both call `ensureInitialized` (:1163) → both cold-start the vector cache → 4(e) single-flight across processes works (one downloads, one waits).
4. Both write `cache/hook-scars.json` (`startup.ts:80`) — same content, torn-read window only.
5. Both share **one** `closing-payload.json` → 3.F: whichever closes second reads the other's reflection or finds the file gone.
6. `currentSession` is per process, so identity does not cross. **Inside** the desktop process, however, a second chat's `session_start` is the 1.1 case: different project → new session, the module slot is overwritten and chat 1 is now operating under chat 2's identity; same project → chat 2 **resumes** chat 1's session (:753-761, `findSessionByHostPid` matches own PID + project).

Net: cross-process is sound for identity, unsound for `threads.json` and `closing-payload.json`; intra-desktop is unsound for identity.

#### 4(b) `session_close` in one process while `recall` in another rewrites the same `session.json`

Two processes bound to one session S arise via:

- **Explicit `session_id`**: `sessionClose` (`session-close.ts:1331`) never checks that S belongs to this process (the registry ownership check exists only on the *fallback* path, :1368-1369). A CLI `bin/gitmem.js session-close` cannot resolve S without an id (its PID owns nothing; `findResumableSessionOnDisk` refuses live foreign PIDs) but with `--session_id`/inline id it proceeds: reads `session.json` (:1346-1357), upserts the row (:1550), `unregisterSession` + `rmSync(sessionDir)` (:496-505). The owning server's next `recall` → `persistSurfacedScars` → `fs.existsSync(sessionFilePath)` is false → **returns silently** (`session-state.ts:407`); same for `setRecallCalled` :276, `setRecallFailure` :304, `confirm-scars.ts:146`, `reflect-scars.ts:128`. Note `getSessionPath` → `getSessionDir` **recreates the empty directory** (`gitmem-dir.ts:334-336`) on every call, so from now on `sessions/S/` exists without `session.json`; `pruneStale` :450-460 treats the registry-less dir via `pruneOrphanedDirs` (deleted after 1 h). The server keeps working from memory; hooks see no live session (`gitmem_live_session_ids` empty) → confirmation gate and Stop-hook enforcement switch off. The server's own later `session_close` re-upserts the row (merge-duplicates, `supabase-client.ts:434`) and overwrites the first close's `close_compliance`/`closing_reflection`.
- **Double adoption**: server A dies; servers B and C both start (e.g. two clients restarting) and both run `findResumableSessionOnDisk` before either has written: both see `pid` dead (:341), both `persistSessionPid(S, self)` (:368, unlocked, atomic rename — last writer wins), both `reconcileRegistryEntry` (:369, locked; second removes the first's entry by `session_id` match :408-411). Both hold `currentSession = S`; both write `session.json` with their own surfaced scars — each `persistSurfacedScars` writes `session.surfacedScars` from **its** memory (`session-state.ts:409`), so the two processes alternately erase each other's scars on disk. First `session_close` deletes the dir; the other continues as above.

Consequence: scar tracking silently becomes memory-only; a second close overwrites the first's compliance record. Reachable but needs an explicit foreign `session_id` or a simultaneous double restart — **MED**.

#### 4(c) SessionStart hook (bash) reads the registry while the server writes it

- `hooks/scripts/session-start.sh` does **not** read the registry or any session file; it only waits for a `gitmem` process (:124-164) and creates `/tmp/gitmem-hooks-<id>` (:170-175). No race.
- `recall-check.sh` (PreToolUse), `post-tool-use.sh`, `session-close-check.sh` (Stop) read `active-sessions.json` through `gitmem_live_session_ids` (`resolve-root.sh:59-87`, `jq`/`node`). The server writes it by rename (2.1), so the hook sees a complete old or new file; the `jq` failure path (`|| return 0`, :66) yields "no session" only on the rename-failure fallback (2.1). Safe.
- `recall-check.sh:43-44` then picks **the newest live session in the registry, not this Claude Code session's** (`head -n 1` over `sort_by(.started_at) | reverse`), and reads its `session.json` (:81-83, `jq … || echo "0"`). With two CLI windows the hook in window 1 reads window 2's `session.json`: the confirmation gate blocks/passes window 1 on window 2's scars. And `session.json` is written non-atomically by the server (2.5-2.7); a hook `jq` that lands mid-write parses garbage → `"0"` for both counts → gate silently open. In the CLI the server's `recall` write completes before the tool result is returned, and the PreToolUse hook fires later, so the overlap requires a *second* process to be writing — again the two-window case.

Consequence: enforcement keyed to the wrong session; torn read = gate off. **MED** (two windows), LOW otherwise.

#### 4(d) `migrate-root` / `migrate-local` (activate) while a server is live

- `migrate-root` (`src/commands/migrate-root.ts:228-290`): `walk` copies every file absent at the destination with `copyFileSync` (:212) — including `sessions/<id>/session.json` from the old project root (not in `isPerInstall` :167-168). A copied session dir with a dead PID and `started_at` < 24 h is then **adopted** by the next `findResumableSessionOnDisk` (`active-sessions.ts:357-370`) in any server that has no in-memory session — a stale session resurrected into a live process. Memory files are read at :239-240 and written by temp+rename at :283-284 minutes later (after conflict planning); a live free-tier server's `create_learning` between read and write is overwritten (the backup at :271-276 preserves the pre-merge destination, **not** the concurrent write). `threads.json` merged by id likewise races 3.A.
- `migrate-local` (called from `activate`, `src/commands/activate.ts:559-569`): reads collections (`migrate-local.ts:200`), POSTs each row, then `archiveLocalData` **renames** `learnings.json` → `learnings.json.pre-migration` (:352). A live free-tier server: `readCollection` → `existsSync` false → `[]` (`local-file-storage.ts:39`) → its next `upsert` creates a new `learnings.json` with one record; on restart the server reads `config.json` with `api_key` → pro → the one stray record stays local and unmigrated (`hasLocalData` on the next `activate` would pick it up, but `activate` is not re-run). Meanwhile the live server's `_tier` is still `free` (1.4) until restart. `activate` also `clearLicenseCache()` (:537, unlink) — harmless to readers.

Consequence: resurrected stale sessions; a learning written during migration ends up in a shadow local file. **MED**, rare.

#### 4(e) Two servers cold-starting → vector cache lock

`src/services/startup.ts:149-231` + `src/services/vector-disk-cache.ts:262-322`:

1. Both fetch the fingerprint (`startup.ts:161`), both miss the cache (:164-172).
2. `tryAcquireDownloadLock` (:178): `openSync(O_CREAT|O_EXCL)` (`vector-disk-cache.ts:278`) — exactly one wins. Loser: if the lock's mtime is >60 s old it unlinks and retries once (:286-289); a lock that vanished between `stat` and `unlink` is retried (:290); otherwise `null`.
3. Loser → `waitForLeaderCache` (:181): polls every 150 ms up to 25 s (:310-321); returns on a fingerprint-matching cache hit, or `null` as soon as the lock disappears without a hit, or on timeout → downloads itself.
4. Leader: delta or full download, `writeVectorCache` (atomic, :242-243) under the fingerprint it observed **before** downloading, then `finally releaseLock()` (:229). A leader that fails returns `scars: []` (:225-227) and still releases → follower sees no lock, no hit → downloads itself. A leader that **crashes** leaves the lock; follower gives up at 25 s and downloads; the lock is broken by the next process after 60 s.
5. Stale-break TOCTOU: two followers both `stat` a >60 s lock, both `unlinkSync`; the second unlink can remove a **fresh** lock created by the first follower after its unlink (:286-288) → third process acquires too → two leaders. Both write atomically; harmless (duplicate download only).
6. Fingerprint skew: the follower's `expected` fingerprint (its own observation) may differ from the leader's if the store changed in between (e.g. `refreshBehavioralScores` RPC from the leader's own `session_start`, `session-start.ts:1169`, bumps `updated_at`) → follower never hits → downloads after the leader releases. Correct, just costly.
7. Same-process double init (1.7): second `initializeGitMem` is a follower to its own process's lock; works, but if the first init ended empty the follower's rows are discarded by `initialize()`'s early return.

Verdict: the cross-process protocol is correct for its stated goal (bounded wait, never stale, never empty-cached). Defects are cost-only, plus the 1.7/1.8 in-process empties. **LOW** for (e) itself.

#### 4(f) `pruneStale` in A deleting a session dir B just created

`src/services/active-sessions.ts:440-541`, under the registry lock:

- Registry entry without `session.json`: pruned (and dir removed) only if `started_at` > 60 s old (:450-461). B writes `session.json` **before** `registerSession` (`session-start.ts:834` then :844), so a registered-but-fileless entry only exists if B's `writeFileSync` failed (caught at :836-838) — then B is pruned at 60 s and B's process keeps a phantom session whose every persist silently no-ops (4(b) behaviour).
- Dir without registry entry (`pruneOrphanedDirs` :496-525): deleted if the **directory** mtime is > 1 h. Directory mtime changes only on entry create/delete/rename — in-place `writeFileSync` of `session.json` (all of 2.5-2.7) does **not** touch it; only `persistSessionPid`'s rename (:386) does. So any session dir older than 1 h whose registry entry is missing is deleted by the next `pruneStale` in **any** process. Registry entries go missing when: (i) `readRegistry` fails validation/parse → `{sessions: []}` (:107-111) and the next locked write persists that empty view (every other live session becomes an orphan dir); (ii) the rename-fallback direct write (:46) is torn; (iii) an explicit-id foreign `session_close` unregisters it (4(b)); (iv) `unregisterSession` by a process that adopted the same session (4(b) double adoption). A live server never repairs its own entry unless `currentSession` is null — `reconcileRegistryEntry` is only reached through `findResumableSessionOnDisk` (:353, :369), which runs only from `checkExistingSession` at `session_start` and from `recoverSessionFromDisk` when memory is empty.
- Age prune: `started_at` > 24 h → entry **and dir** deleted regardless of PID liveness (:463-470). A desktop-app session, or a CLI session left open overnight, is destroyed by the first `session_start` anywhere on the machine after 24 h; the owning process then silently loses persistence (4(b)) and its eventual `session_close` finds no local file and, if the Supabase row is missing/free tier, fails with "Session … not found" (:1383-1392) or writes a bare record (3.E).
- B's brand-new dir is safe from the 1 h rule; the 60 s rule is safe because file precedes registration. The unsafe cases are lost registry entries and >24 h sessions.

Consequence: a live session's `session.json` (surfaced scars, confirmations, reflections, threads) deleted from under it; all later persistence silently no-ops. **HIGH** (24 h path is routine for desktop; registry-reset path is one torn read away).

#### 4(g) `registerSession` displacement rule (hostname+pid+project)

`active-sessions.ts:137-139, 148-175`: an entry with a different `session_id` but the same host+pid+project is removed and reported as displaced; `session_start` then PATCHes it `superseded` in Supabase (`session-start.ts:853-855, 481-507`).

- Same process, same project, new id: only via `force: true` (`checkExistingSession` returns null at :737-740; otherwise the existing session is resumed). Intended.
- **PID reuse**: after a reboot or a process exit, a new server can receive the PID of a dead session's owner. `findSessionByHostPid(host, pid, project)` (:754) matches the dead entry → "Resuming own session" (:759) → the new process **adopts a stale session** (≤24 h old, survived `pruneStale` because dead PIDs are deliberately kept, :473-475) with its surfaced scars/threads/`started_at`; the duration clock and close ceremony then belong to the old session. `findResumableSessionOnDisk` has the same hole: `pid === currentPid` takes the "mine" path (:351) with no `started_at`-vs-process-start check. macOS/Linux PID reuse within 24 h on a busy dev machine is plausible.
- Different project, same process: left open by design (GIT-86) — this is what makes 1.1 possible: two open sessions per process but one `currentSession` slot.
- `hostname` is part of the slot but `findResumableSessionOnDisk` also refuses other hosts (:320); a synced/backup-restored `~/.gitmem` is safe.

**MED** (PID reuse), otherwise as designed.

#### 4(h) Hooks' `/tmp/gitmem-hooks-$SESSION_ID`

`SESSION_ID="${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID:-$$}"` in `session-start.sh:170`, `recall-check.sh:105`, `post-tool-use.sh:102`, `session-close-check.sh:24`. `$$` is the PID of **that hook's bash process** — a fresh value on every invocation.

- If `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` is **not** exported to hook processes (the scripts never read the `session_id` field of the stdin JSON, which is where Claude Code delivers it): every hook invocation gets its own state dir. `tool_call_count` is always 1 → the recall nag (needs >10, `recall-check.sh:191`) never fires; `session-close-check.sh:48-58` finds no `STATE_DIR`, creates one, exits 0 → the Stop-hook "session still open" block **never fires**; `stop_hook_active` is never consulted. `/tmp` accumulates one dir per tool call (cleaned only by `uninstall-hooks`, `bin/gitmem.js:837-851`).
- If it **is** exported: state is per Claude session, correctly isolated across windows. Then `session-close-check.sh:27-30` blocks at most once per session (`stop_hook_active`), and `:109-111` removes the dir once the registry is empty — but "registry empty" is evaluated machine-wide (`gitmem_live_session_ids`), so with two windows open the Stop hook in window 1 blocks because window 2's session is still registered, and after its one allowed block it never cleans up.
- `/tmp/gitmem-hooks-*` and `/tmp/gitmem-hooks-plugin-debug.log` (`session-start.sh:11`) are world-visible fixed paths; on a multi-user host a pre-existing dir/file owned by another user makes `echo "0" > …` fail under `set -e` → hook exits non-zero.

**MED** (enforcement either dead or cross-window, depending on the env var), LOW security.

Both are `POST … Prefer: resolution=merge-duplicates` (`src/services/supabase-client.ts:434`), i.e. `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` of **every column sent** — full-row last-writer-wins, no version check. The close does not await `pendingSessionRows` (only `recordMetrics` does, `metrics.ts:194`). | Create request delayed/retried past the close's upsert (fast `quick` close, automation, network reorder); or `session_close` reaches Supabase while the create is still in flight so `getRecord` (:1335) 404s → falls back to the local file (:1346-1357) and builds the row from `session.json` → after both land, the fields the create carries win if it lands last. | Closed session reverts to `close_compliance: null`, `closing_reflection: null`, `decisions: []`, `open_threads: []` — invisible to `loadLastSession` (filters `close_compliance != null`, session-start.ts:243) and to `aggregateThreads`; the reflection is gone (threads survive in the threads table). Also `markSessionSuperseded` (:481-507, fire-and-forget PATCH) can land after the displaced session's own close in another chat and overwrite its `close_compliance` with `superseded`. | Occasionally (short sessions / tests / flaky network) | MED |

### 6. `src/services/file-lock.ts` review

Read in full (152 lines).

| aspect | finding | file:line |
|---|---|---|
| Atomic create | `fs.openSync(lockPath, O_CREAT \| O_EXCL \| O_WRONLY)` — correct on local POSIX filesystems and NTFS; `O_EXCL` is not honoured by NFSv2/v3 servers that lack the `EXCLUSIVE` create mode, so a networked `~` (NFS/SMB home, some container volume mounts) gives no mutual exclusion. Not detected or documented. | :69 |
| Holder identity | `{pid, hostname, acquired_at}` written **after** create; a crash between `openSync` and `writeSync` leaves an empty lock → `isLockStale` parse fails → treated as stale → broken immediately (:45-47). Benign (microsecond window). | :70-76, :39-49 |
| Reentrance | Same `pid`+`hostname` → returns without taking or counting the lock (:84-91). The inner `withLockSync`'s `finally` then **unlinks the outer's lock** (:149-151) while the outer critical section is still running; the outer's own release hits ENOENT and is ignored (:134-136). No current call site nests (`registerSession`/`unregisterSession`/`reconcileRegistryEntry`/`pruneStale`/`migrateFromLegacy` never call each other), so latent. PID reuse across a reboot (lock file left by a dead process whose PID the new one inherited) is misread as reentrant → lock skipped; the 30 s stale rule would have broken it anyway. | :84-91, :145-152 |
| Stale detection | Wall-clock age of `acquired_at` > 30 s (:16, :43-44). Critical sections are milliseconds, so a 30 s holder is a crash — except `sleepSync`/slow disks in the desktop app. Clock skew between processes on one host is nil; NTP step during the window would break a live lock. | :16, :39-49 |
| Stale-break TOCTOU | `isLockStale` (read) → `unlinkSync` (:97-99) are not atomic: waiter W1 unlinks the stale file and creates a fresh lock; waiter W2, which also judged it stale before W1's create, unlinks **W1's fresh lock** (the unlink error branch at :102-104 only catches ENOENT). A third waiter (or W2's own retry) then creates a lock while W1 believes it holds it → two holders → both `readRegistry`/`writeRegistry` → one registration lost (last rename wins). Requires a crashed holder plus ≥2 concurrent waiters — e.g. a fan-out of N sub-agent servers starting within 5 s of a crashed one. | :96-105 |
| Release on exception | `withLockSync` uses `try/finally` → yes. | :145-152 |
| Release on crash | No (advisory file); recovered by the stale rule after 30 s. | — |
| Timeout behaviour | 5 s of 25 ms polls, then **throws** (:107-119). Callers: `registerSession` throws out of `writeSessionFiles` → caught at `session-start.ts:1219-1223` → **session file exists but the session is never registered** (hooks see no session; `pruneOrphanedDirs` deletes its dir after 1 h — 4(f)). `pruneStale`/`migrateFromLegacy` throw inside `checkExistingSession` → caught at :768-770 → returns `null` → **a new session is created even though this process already owns one** (duplicate session for the same PID+project; the old one is later displaced or pruned). `unregisterSession` throws inside `cleanupSessionFiles` → caught :497 → dir still removed → registry keeps a dangling entry (pruned after 60 s). `reconcileRegistryEntry` throws → caught :417-420. So a single crashed lock holder makes every `session_start` on the machine for the next 30 s open a phantom/duplicate session. | :107-119; `session-start.ts:742-770, 1219-1223`; `session-close.ts:493-499` |
| Blocking | `sleepSync` uses `Atomics.wait` on the main thread (:30-34) — permitted in Node, blocks the whole event loop for up to 5 s: the desktop app's other chats and all in-flight fire-and-forget promises stall. | :30-34, :121 |
| Cross-process scope | Lock path is `<gitmemDir>/active-sessions.lock` (`active-sessions.ts:23, 87-89`) → shared by every process on the machine using that root. Correct scope for the registry. | — |
| Windows | `O_EXCL` works; `unlinkSync` of the closed lock file works; `hostname()` stable. `renameSync` in `atomicWriteFileSync` over an existing file works on Node (uses `MoveFileEx` with replace). No known Windows-specific defect. | — |
| Tests | `tests/unit/services/file-lock.test.ts` covers acquire/release, stale-break (30 s and corrupt), reentrance, timeout, `withLockSync` exception path. `tests/unit/services/active-sessions-locking.test.ts` runs **sequential** register/unregister/prune in one process ("simulated concurrency", :82) — there is no multi-process test, no test of the stale-break TOCTOU, no test of the timeout-throw consequences in `session_start`. | — |

**Call sites that use `withLockSync`:** `active-sessions.ts:150, 182, 402, 441, 563`.

**Sites that RMW a shared JSON store and should hold a lock but do not:** `thread-manager.ts:271` (+ callers 3.A), `local-file-storage.ts:141, 152, 231`, `thread-suggestions.ts:213` (+ callers 3.D), `doc-index.ts:102`, `active-sessions.ts:386` (`persistSessionPid`, called outside the lock at :368), every `session.json` RMW when two processes are bound to one session (3.B), `migrate-root.ts:283-287`, `startup.ts:80`, `license.ts:132`, `session-close.ts:976-998/1695` (`closing-payload.json`, which needs per-session pathing more than a lock).

---

### Counts

- **Shared mutable module-level state items:** 20 (table 1), of which 3 HIGH (`currentSession`, `_tier` + its cached derivatives, vector index emptied by failed refresh), 4 MED, 13 LOW.
- **File write sites:** 31 rows in table 2; atomic temp+rename **3** helpers (covering 2 stores + migration output); direct-overwrite **~30** sites across **11** parsed JSON stores; append **6**; delete **7**.
- **Readers that turn a torn read into a permanent overwrite ("starting fresh"):** 5 (`local-file-storage.ts:44`, `thread-manager.ts:257`, `thread-suggestions.ts:198`, `active-sessions.ts:107-111`, `doc-index.ts:79`).
- **Lock-free RMW groups on JSON stores:** 11 (table 3); 2 HIGH (`closing-payload.json`, free-tier collections), 3 MED, 6 LOW.
- **In-process races:** 11 (table 5); 2 HIGH, 5 MED, 4 LOW.
- **`withLockSync` call sites:** 5, all on the registry. **Unlocked RMW sites that touch cross-process stores:** 10 groups.
- **file-lock defects:** 1 correctness (stale-break TOCTOU), 1 latent (reentrance releases outer lock), 1 environmental (NFS), 1 blast-radius (timeout → duplicate/phantom sessions), 1 liveness (5 s main-thread block).

### Ranked top 10

| rank | issue | where | why it ranks here |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | One `currentSession` slot per process while the desktop app serves many chats: scars, confirmations, threads, observations and project scope cross between sessions; `session_close` in one chat clears the other's state and records the other's scars under its own id. | `session-state.ts:56`; `session-start.ts:1230, 753-761, 1104-1110`; `session-close.ts:581-608, 763, 788, 1677`; `recall.ts:425, 580, 688` | Wrong data written to durable stores (`scar_usage`, session rows) in a supported deployment; partially acknowledged in code but only the `force` path handles it. |
| 2 | `closing-payload.json` is a single machine-wide file shared by every session; two concurrent sessions read/overwrite/delete each other's closing reflection. | `session-close.ts:976-998, 1695`; `resolve-root.sh:44`; `session-close-check.sh:97-101`; `init-wizard.js:454` | Reflection/decisions/threads from session B stored on session A's row; B told its payload is missing. Two open Claude Code windows is the common case. |
| 3 | `pruneStale` destroys live sessions: any session > 24 h old, or any session whose registry entry was lost (one torn registry read → `{sessions: []}` → the next locked write persists it), has its `session.json` deleted by the next `session_start` in **any** process; the owning process then no-ops every persist silently. | `active-sessions.ts:107-111, 463-470, 496-525`; `session-state.ts:276, 304, 407`; `confirm-scars.ts:146`; `session-start.ts:834` | Silent, cross-process, destructive; the long-lived desktop app crosses 24 h routinely. |
| 4 | Free tier: whole-collection direct overwrite + lock-free RMW + "starting fresh" on parse error. Two windows lose learnings/decisions/session rows; one torn read wipes `learnings.json` on the next write. | `local-file-storage.ts:37-47, 62-66, 132-141`; `session-start.ts:584`; `session-close.ts:289-303` | The free tier's only store; loss is permanent and unreported. |
| 5 | Periodic refresh / `cache_flush` network failure empties the in-memory vector index **and** overwrites `hook-scars.json` with `[]`, reports `success: true`, and `ensureInitialized` never re-initializes. | `startup.ts:225-227, 359-365, 677-683, 80, 802-815`; `local-vector-search.ts:119, 129-137, 175-179` | Recall silently returns nothing for the rest of the session on a transient error; happens on a 15-minute timer. |
| 6 | Async license validation flips `_tier` to `free` after tools, config, storage and possibly a session were set up as pro. | `server.ts:499-508`; `tier.ts:73`; `config.ts:141`; `storage.ts:143`; `session-start.ts:1119, 1149`; `session-close.ts:1200` | Session opened in Supabase, closed to local JSON; subsequent writes go local while the user believes they are on pro; warning is stderr-only. Triggered by any 72 h-expired cache + unreachable license server. |
| 7 | `threads.json` lock-free RMW, and `resolve_thread`/`session_close` writing the in-memory subset over the file. | `thread-manager.ts:271`; `session-start.ts:862-894`; `create-thread.ts:319-321`; `resolve-thread.ts:70-73, 196`; `session-close.ts:1414-1450` | Cross-process thread loss; on free tier the file is the store. |
| 8 | `createSessionRecord` fire-and-forget and `session_close` are both full-row `merge-duplicates` upserts with no ordering guarantee; a late create reverts a closed session to unclosed and drops its reflection. | `session-start.ts:444-457, 1149`; `session-close.ts:1550`; `supabase-client.ts:434`; `metrics.ts:194` (only metrics wait) | Silent loss of the closing reflection; the same pattern (`markSessionSuperseded` PATCH) can overwrite another chat's close. |
| 9 | `file-lock` timeout throws → `checkExistingSession` opens a **duplicate** session for a process that already owns one, and `registerSession` failure leaves a session file with no registry entry (→ #3 after 1 h); stale-break TOCTOU allows two holders. | `file-lock.ts:96-119`; `session-start.ts:742-770, 1219-1223`; `session-close.ts:493-499` | A single crashed lock holder degrades every `session_start` on the machine for 30 s into phantom/duplicate sessions. |

Honourable mentions (LOW, but real): `session.json` non-atomic writes read by hook `jq` → `"0"` → gate off (2.5, 4(c)); `behavioral-decay.ts:17-19` reads env only so config.json-based pro installs never refresh decay (1.19); `bin/gitmem.js init`/`init-scars` writes `process.cwd()/.gitmem/config.json` and `learnings.json` — a root the server no longer reads (2.21, 2.24); `bin/gitmem.js session-start` (:371-391) still creates a session under a CLI PID that exits immediately, leaving a dead-PID session for the next server to adopt (4(g)).

## 8. Class F — Tool Contract Review (gitmem v1.11.0, f93c3a3, branch audit/GIT-113-phase-0)


Read-only audit. All paths relative to `/Users/chriscrawford/nTEG-Labs/gitmem`. "Customer store" = Supabase project provisioned from `schema/setup.sql` (pro tier). "Free" = local JSON in `.gitmem/`.

### Cross-cutting facts (apply to many rows)

- **Dispatch (src/server.ts:429-435):** client receives ONLY `result.display` if it is a string; else `result.text`; else `JSON.stringify(result)`. Every result field not rendered into `display` never reaches the agent. `durable` / `stored_in` (GIT-101, src/services/write-result.ts:34-42) are returned by write tools but **no tool renders them into `display`** (verified per row below).
- **Tool registration is computed once at server creation** (src/server.ts:119-120) with the optimistic tier (src/services/tier.ts:38-45: key present + no cache ⇒ "pro"). Async license validation may later `setTier("free")` (src/server.ts:500-502) but `registeredTools` is not recomputed, so a downgraded server still advertises pro-only tools (`analyze`, `graph_traverse`, cache tools) whose handlers then run on the free path.
- **Zod validation (src/schemas/registry.ts:35-55)** exists for 19 of 30 canonical tools. Zod objects are non-strict, and the server passes the ORIGINAL `toolArgs` to the handler (src/server.ts:145,167), so unknown keys are neither rejected nor stripped. Tools with no Zod schema: confirm_scars, reflect_scars, session_refresh, create_thread, promote_suggestion, dismiss_suggestion, cleanup_threads, graph_traverse, health, index_docs, search_docs.
- **setup.sql does NOT create:** any RPC named `match_*` (only `gitmem_semantic_search` :287 and `gitmem_scar_search` :322), `transcript_chunks`, `match_transcript_chunks`, a storage bucket, `variant_assignments`, `variant_performance_metrics`, `scar_enforcement_variants.active`, `gitmem_feedback`, sessions columns `task_observations` / `children` / `claude_code_session_id` (src/services/session-columns.ts:75-87 lists 11 production-only columns).
- **RPC name mismatch (customer-store breaking):** src/services/supabase-client.ts:190 calls `match_gitmem_learnings`, :693 calls `match_gitmem_learnings_weighted`. Neither exists in setup.sql. Every code path that reaches these RPCs on a customer store gets PGRST202. (GIT-93's comment at :680-688 says the survey was of "deployed functions" — the internal store — not setup.sql.)
- **Local vector index (pro):** `isLocalSearchReady()` is `initialized && scars.length > 0` (src/services/local-vector-search.ts:204). Index is cross-project and ignores `project` (src/services/local-vector-search.ts:369-378, `_project` unused). Entries without an embedding are not indexed (src/services/startup.ts:249 comment; supabase-client `INDEXED_LEARNING_FILTERS`).
- **Embedding provider absent (pro):** `embed()` returns `null` (src/services/embedding.ts:261-263); `scarSearch`/`semanticSearch` then return `[]` silently (supabase-client.ts:675-678, :183-186); `LocalVectorSearch._embed` throws (local-vector-search.ts:262-264).

#### Tier-gated / dev-only claims summary (tools)
- `hasVariants`, `hasTranscripts`, `hasBatchOperations`, `hasCompliance`, `hasEnforcementFields` are all `tier === "dev"` (tier.ts:112-135). Customer-facing descriptions that still mention these: recall (`issue_id` variants), record_scar_usage (`variant_id`), session_close docstring (compliance validation, transcripts).
- `hasProInsights`/`hasMetrics` are pro+dev but depend on `gitmem_query_metrics` / `gitmem_scar_usage` rows that only accumulate when the fire-and-forget writes succeed.

---

### Part 2 — CLI commands (bin/gitmem.js, bin/init-wizard.js, bin/uninstall.js, src/commands/*.ts)

**Root mismatch (applies to several rows):** the server, hooks (`hooks/scripts/resolve-root.sh`) and `migrate-root` resolve the store as `GITMEM_DIR` → `GITMEM_HOME/.gitmem` → `~/.gitmem` (src/services/gitmem-dir.ts:57-97, CHANGELOG:197). The CLI commands `init` (bin/init-wizard.js:25,169), `init-scars` (bin/gitmem.js:122), `install-hooks` (bin/gitmem.js:532), `telemetry` (src/commands/telemetry.ts:26-28) and `uninstall` (bin/uninstall.js:125) use `process.cwd()/.gitmem`.

| command | what --help/docs promise | prints on success | verifiable | gap | severity |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **init** (bin/init-wizard.js) | "Interactive setup wizard (recommended)"; README:35 "`.gitmem/` directory with starter scars"; footer "installed successfully!" | Per-step ✔ lines: "Created .gitmem/ — your local memory store / N lessons…", "Configured MCP server", "Created CLAUDE.md", "Auto-approved gitmem tools", "Added automatic memory hooks", "Updated .gitignore", AGENTS.md, feedback opt-in; then "gitmem installed successfully!" | Writes `<cwd>/.gitmem/{config.json,learnings.json,threads.json,sessions.json,decisions.json,scar-usage.json,closing-payload.json,closing-payload-template.json,hooks/}` (:169, :396-548). Does not start the server or verify anything. | (1) **Writes the memory store to `<cwd>/.gitmem`, which the server does not read** (server root is `~/.gitmem`, gitmem-dir.ts:93-95). Starter scars, `config.json.project`, and the closing-payload template land in a root the server treats as *stranded*; the very next `session_start` prints "Memory store found but NOT being read … npx gitmem-mcp migrate-root" (session-start.ts:966-983). "installed successfully" is untrue for the memory-store step on the free tier. (2) `init` writes a pre-filled `closing-payload.json` with empty answers and a `task_completion` of nulls (:452-479); if it were in the read root, session_close would consume it as a real payload (`payloadConsumed=true`, session-close.ts:984-992) with blank reflections. (3) Writes `feedback_enabled:false, telemetry_enabled:false` into config; `telemetry_enabled` is never read by the server (no `getTelemetry` call outside the CLI). (4) `--project` sets config in the unread root. (5) Hook set installed here includes `UserPromptSubmit → auto-retrieve-hook.sh` (:286-296) which `install-hooks` does not install — the two installers disagree. (6) `buildMcpConfig` writes a placeholder `"<your-openai-key-or-remove>"` into `.mcp.json` when `SUPABASE_URL` is set but `OPENAI_API_KEY` is not (:265-266) — a literal placeholder becomes the embedding key. | (1) High; (2)(5)(6) Medium; (3)(4) Low |
| **init-scars** (legacy `cmdInit`, bin/gitmem.js:100-307) | Not in help. Free: "initializing free tier (local storage)"; pro: "Loading N starter scars into Supabase". | "Done: N new scars added to .gitmem/learnings.json" or "Done: N loaded, M failed" + "Starter scars are ready!". | Free: `<cwd>/.gitmem` (unread root). Pro: POSTs each scar with `Prefer: return=representation` to `${prefix}learnings` — HTTP status only; starter-scars.json fields are sent verbatim (no column filtering). | (1) Same root mismatch. (2) Pro path reads env vars only (not config.json). (3) "Starter scars are ready! … recall(…)" — on pro, starter scars have no embedding (none generated) so recall's local index will never include them (index requires embedding). | (1)(3) Medium |
| **setup** (bin/gitmem.js:85-98) | "Output SQL for Supabase schema setup (pro/dev tier)"; header comments "Copy and paste this SQL into your Supabase SQL Editor". | The full `schema/setup.sql` to stdout. | Applies nothing, verifies nothing (by design). | (1) Prints the **license-management section too** (setup.sql:504-665 — `gitmem_licenses`, `gitmem_license_activations`, `gitmem_validate_license`, `gitmem_deactivate_device`) which `activate` deliberately strips (activate.ts:172-176); a customer pasting the output creates gitmem's admin tables and a SECURITY DEFINER RPC in their own project. (2) The SQL it prints does not define the RPCs the server calls (`match_gitmem_learnings*`), so "schema setup" leaves semantic fallback and `search` broken on a cold index. | (1) Medium; (2) High (root cause tracked above) |
| **activate** (src/commands/activate.ts) | "Activate Pro tier (license key + credentials wizard)"; docstring "4. Test Supabase connection (verify tables exist)". | "✓ Key validated (pro tier)", "✓ Connected to Supabase", "✓ Schema verified (all tables present)" or "✓ Schema applied automatically", OpenRouter status, migration counts, "Pro tier activated! Restart your editor to apply." + config path. | Validates key via remote RPC (device registered). `checkSchemaExists` probes **4 tables only** (`gitmem_learnings/sessions/decisions/scar_usage`, :105) — not threads, knowledge_triples, query_metrics, views or RPC functions. (7) Migration `TEXT_COERCE_FIELDS` (migrate-local.ts:116-120) confirms the schema/code type drift for `action_protocol`/`self_check_criteria`. | (2) High; (1)(3)(4)(5) Medium; (6)(7) Low | **[Corrected in §11: `pg` IS a dependency, package.json:42 — not a finding.]**

| **server** / default (bin/gitmem.js:922-934) | "Start MCP server (default)". | stderr: `[gitmem] Tier: … | Storage: … | Tools: N`, cache/licence lines, write-path verdict (write-health.ts). | Registration computed once with the optimistic tier (see cross-cutting). | (1) Tool list not recomputed after license downgrade. (2) All durability/tier warnings are stderr-only; `health` is the only in-band surface. | Medium |
| **help** | Usage text (bin/gitmem.js:35-83). | — | — | Omits `init-scars`, `session-start`, `session-refresh`, `telemetry show/clear`; the "Pro Tier (with Supabase)" quick start (:76-81) describes the env-var path and `init`, not `activate`, and step 4 says "init (auto-detects pro tier)" — `init-wizard` only inserts env vars into `.mcp.json`, it does not detect or validate a tier. | Low |

---

### Summary 1 — Top 10 gaps, ranked

1. **`init` provisions the memory store in a root the server never reads.** `bin/init-wizard.js:25,169` writes `<cwd>/.gitmem/{learnings.json,config.json,threads.json,closing-payload.json}`; the server, hooks and `migrate-root` resolve `~/.gitmem` (`src/services/gitmem-dir.ts:93-95`, `hooks/scripts/resolve-root.sh:31-36`). The first `session_start` after a fresh free-tier install reports the just-created directory as "Memory store found but NOT being read" (`src/tools/session-start.ts:966-983`). Same defect in `init-scars` (`bin/gitmem.js:122`), `telemetry` (`src/commands/telemetry.ts:26-28`), `uninstall --all` (`bin/uninstall.js:125`).
2. **Semantic-search RPCs the server calls do not exist in `schema/setup.sql`.** `src/services/supabase-client.ts:190` → `match_gitmem_learnings`; `:693` → `match_gitmem_learnings_weighted`; setup.sql defines only `gitmem_semantic_search` (:287) and `gitmem_scar_search` (:322). On a customer store: recall (cold index) shows an error banner; `search`, `prepare_context`'s fallback and `SupabaseStorage.search` (storage.ts:87-97) render "No results found." / `[]`.
3. **`search` and `log` render every failure as an empty result** (`src/tools/search.ts:218-230, 350-363`; `src/tools/log.ts:191-200, 283-294`). Combined with (2), a customer with a cold index sees "No results found." for a broken RPC. `recall` and `prepare_context` disclose errors; these two do not.
4. **Pro tier without an embedding provider is silently non-functional for retrieval.** `create_learning` stores rows with no `embedding` and prints "Created" (`src/tools/create-learning.ts:213-215, 322`); the local index skips them (`startup.ts:249`, `INDEXED_LEARNING_FILTERS`) and the RPC path returns `[]` (`supabase-client.ts:675-678`). `recall` then reports "No relevant scars found" and `clearRecallFailure()` (`recall.ts:572`) lets `confirm_scars` answer "Proceed freely" (`confirm-scars.ts:222`). `activate` merely warns "Skipped (semantic search will not work…)" (`activate.ts:520`).
5. **`session_close`'s advertised schema is a fraction of what it reads, and its stats are self-reported.** `definitions.ts:180-210` lists 6 params; Zod (`src/schemas/session-close.ts`) and the handler also read `decisions`, `open_threads`, `project_state`, `learnings_created`, `scars_to_record`, `task_completion`, `capture_transcript`, `transcript_path`, `close_type:"retroactive"`. "N learnings" = `params.learnings_created.length` (`session-close.ts:1308`); "N scars applied" counted before the fire-and-forget batch write (`:378`, `:1606-1611`) whose failure cannot appear in this close's display (`:1727`). `durable`/`stored_in` never rendered.
6. **`check` passes a broken store.** "recall: Recall infrastructure operational" and "write: Write operations functional" test the local result cache only (`src/commands/check.ts:278-314`); Supabase creds are read from env only (`:81-82`), prefix hard-coded (`:145,:354,:452`), embedding key from env only (`:83-99`). A config.json-activated Pro user gets "Supabase connection failed … undefined/rest/v1" while `hasSupabase()` is true.
7. **`install-hooks` / `uninstall-hooks` destroy non-gitmem hooks.** `bin/gitmem.js:659` assigns `settings.hooks = gitmemHooks` wholesale (no merge); `:806` `delete cfg.hooks` removes every Claude Code hook. `init-wizard.js:762-771` and `bin/uninstall.js` merge/filter correctly — the standalone commands do not.
8. **`session_start` display omits what the description promises and what `session_close` needs.** `last_session` is loaded but not rendered (`session-start.ts:919-1065`, used only at `:1178-1181`); `gitmem_dir` is not rendered although `session_close`'s description (`definitions.ts:179`) tells the agent to write `{gitmem_dir}/closing-payload.json`. Session-row insert is fire-and-forget with `stored` discarded (`:1149-1152`); failed `sessions_lite`/thread reads are stderr-only (`:204,:211`) and render as "No threads or decisions."
9. **Dead features advertised in tool descriptions.** `promote_suggestion`/`dismiss_suggestion` reference "session_start's suggested_threads list" (`definitions.ts:734`) that no tool produces (`session-start.ts:45`; only `types/index.ts:192` mentions it). `recall` advertises `Full body: search("<id>")` (`recall.ts:387`) but `search` has no id lookup (`search.ts`; BM25 docs index title/keywords/description only, `local-file-storage.ts:166-172`). `telemetry` CLI says "Events are logged automatically as you use gitmem tools" (`telemetry.ts:181`) but nothing in `src/server.ts`/`src/index.ts`/tools imports `getTelemetry`.
10. **`activate`'s DATABASE_URL schema path cannot work as shipped.** `src/commands/activate.ts:193` `await import("pg")`; `package.json` dependencies are `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`, `uuid`, `zod` only. The wizard advertises `DATABASE_URL` as an automatic option (`:465-468`). Its "Schema verified (all tables present)" (`:476`) probes 4 tables (`:105`) and no views/RPCs. **[Corrected in §11: `pg` IS a dependency, package.json:42 — not a finding.]**

Honourable mentions (Medium): `absorb_observations` "persist" is memory-only on every customer store and free (`absorb-observations.ts:66-80`, `session-columns.ts:107-121`, `session-close.ts:223-344`); `archive_learning` fabricates `archived_at` on customer stores (`archive-learning.ts:132-136`; column absent from setup.sql); `resolve_thread` writes triples with `project:"default"` hard-coded (`resolve-thread.ts:246,259`); `session_refresh` returns raw JSON on free tier (`session-start.ts:1368-1376`); tool registration frozen at optimistic tier (`server.ts:119-124`, `:500-502`); `deactivate` prints "Device removed from license server" unconditionally (`deactivate.ts:105`); `setup` prints the license-admin SQL section (`bin/gitmem.js:93`, `setup.sql:504-665`).

## 9. CLASS G — TEST BLINDNESS

gitmem @ `audit/GIT-113-phase-0` = `origin/main` f93c3a3 (v1.11.0). Read-only audit, 2026-09-22.

### 0. Method and ground facts

**Inputs.** `analysis.json` (from `analyze.mjs`): TypeScript-AST scan of 114 `src/**/*.ts` and 132 `tests/**/*.{ts,mjs}`; per-test import graph, `vi.mock` factories (186 calls in 33 files; 178 resolve to a `src/` module), reachability through the src graph stopping at mocked modules, and a factory-vs-real-exports diff. Spot-checked by hand: line counts (`wc -l` = JSON `lines`−1 for every file checked, JSON counts the trailing newline), the `session-close-bridge.test.ts:165` active-sessions factory, the three `vi.stubGlobal("fetch")` sites, and every "live omitted" call site listed in §3.

**Refinement applied.** The analyzer marks a namespace import (`import * as supabase from "./supabase-client.js"`) as importing *every* export, so its `liveOmitted` over-reports (e.g. 24 "live" names for `metrics.ts`). `refine-live.mjs` re-scans each reachable module for actual `ns.name` references / named-import uses. §3 uses the refined numbers: 147 factories omit ≥1 real export; **21 factories** omit an export that reachable code-under-test actually references.

**Empirical probe.** The 14 unit files affected by those 21 factories were run once (`vitest run`, no coverage, cache dir and `cacheDir` pointed at the scratchpad, `GITMEM_HOME` tmp per the repo config). Result: 14 files / 144 tests pass; repo untouched (`git status --porcelain` line count and `node_modules/.vite/vitest/*` mtime identical before/after). vitest's `No "<x>" export is defined on the "<m>" mock` message appeared **21 times, all for `getSessionDir`**, all swallowed by a `try/catch` in `session-close.ts` — see §3.

**What actually runs in CI** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`):

| job | what | trigger |
|---|---|---|
| `build` (node 18/20/22) | `typecheck`, `build`, `npm run test:unit` (`tests/unit/**/*.test.ts`, 94 files, 1,272 `it(`), `test:smoke:free` (`tests/smoke/smoke-free.test.ts`, 3 tests, spawns `dist/index.js` on GITMEM_TIER=free), one e2e file `tests/e2e/git-89-session-identity.test.ts` (free tier, spawns dist), depcheck | push main / PR / tags |
| `hooks-without-jq` | `bash hooks/tests/test-hooks.sh` in `node:20-bookworm-slim` with **no jq and no ps** (48 `pass` assertions) | same |
| `venue-gate` | `scripts/venue-schema.sh` + `node tests/e2e/blank-supabase.mjs flow|egress|projects` against a disposable real Supabase (pro tier) on v1.8.0 then current `schema/setup.sql` | **v\* tags and `workflow_dispatch` only** |
| `publish` | needs all three | tags |

**Not in CI at all:** `tests/integration/**` (5 files, 63 tests, Docker testcontainers), `tests/e2e/**` except git-89 (8 other files), `tests/smoke/smoke-pro.test.ts` (needs live `SUPABASE_URL`), `tests/performance/**` (42 benches), `tests/compliance/mcp-protocol-compliance.mjs`, `hooks/tests/test-hooks.sh` **with** jq (only the no-jq variant runs).

**Two facts that reframe the non-unit tiers:**
1. `tests/integration/*.test.ts` import **nothing from `src/`**. They run raw `pg` SQL against a pgvector container (`tests/integration/setup.ts:101-104` sets `SUPABASE_URL` to a `postgres://` URI). The `describe("session_start")` / `describe("session_close")` blocks in `session-lifecycle.test.ts` are labels around hand-written `INSERT`/`UPDATE` statements ("simulating session_start component", line 61). They test `schema/setup.sql`, not gitmem.
2. `tests/e2e/pro-fresh.test.ts` and `pro-mature.test.ts` (Docker-gated) hand the spawned server `SUPABASE_URL = container.getConnectionUri()` — a `postgres://` URI (pro-fresh.test.ts:84). `supabase-client.ts` speaks PostgREST over `fetch`, so every store call fails; the suite's own comment (pro-fresh.test.ts:130-132) says recall "may fail with URL error. Key assertion: the tool doesn't crash". Assertions are `isToolError === false` and `text.length > 0`. These suites exercise the **fail-open** path of pro tier, not the store.

So the only places gitmem's Supabase boundary is exercised for real are: (a) `tests/e2e/blank-supabase.mjs` on tags/dispatch; (b) `tests/smoke/smoke-pro.test.ts` when someone runs it with live credentials (it does verify the session row via REST: `querySessionRow`, lines 31-33, 99-100); (c) three unit files with a `vi.stubGlobal("fetch")` (§5a).

**Class key for §1.** a = direct tests with the risky layer REAL (temp dir for fs; `vi.stubGlobal("fetch")` with `supabase-client.ts` unmocked for HTTP); a\* = the HTTP layer is real only as a *throwing* stub (write-result-contract.test.ts:91-95 throws `TypeError` on every fetch — exercises the failure branch, never a request/response shape); b = direct tests but the risky layer mocked in all of them; c = transitive only; d = zero unit coverage. "pure logic" = no fs/HTTP/process boundary in the file.

Counts from the classifier (hand-overridden rows noted inline): **a 61 · b 16 · c 15 · d 22** (of 114). 14 files are not reachable from `src/server.ts` at all (commands/\*, diagnostics/\*, hooks/quick-retrieve, lib/telemetry, schemas/index, types/index) — they can only be exercised by CLI/hook paths, none of which run in CI except via `test-hooks.sh` (bash) and the smoke/e2e spawns of `dist/index.js`.

Notes on §1: "direct test files" are `tests/unit/...` unless prefixed (`performance/`, `setup/`). "mocked layers" is the union of `vi.mock` targets across that file's direct tests — a layer listed there is mocked in at least one direct test, not necessarily all; the class column already accounts for whether any direct test leaves the risky layer real. The e2e column is derived from the src import closure of the tools each real-run suite calls (CI free-tier: `session_start`, `session_close`, `recall`, `create_learning` from git-89 + smoke-free; venue-gate: `session_start`, `list_threads`, `recall`, `confirm_scars`, `create_thread`, `resolve_thread`, `session_close` from `blank-supabase.mjs` flow mode), so "module loaded" means the file is imported by a called tool, not that its branches ran.

### 3. The findSessionById shape — mock factories that omit exports (completed by the lead auditor)

From the static diff of all 178 `vi.mock` factories against the real modules' exports (`analyze.mjs` → `analysis.json`): **168 factories replace the module outright** (10 spread `importOriginal`), **147 omit at least one export**, and **27 omit an export that code reachable from the test's subject imports**. A factory omission only throws when the export is *called*, so "reachable import" is an upper bound; the rows below are narrowed by hand.

**Observed live, right now (the findSessionById case recurring):** running `session-close-payload-path.test.ts` (and the other session-close suites) prints `[session_close] Failed to clean up session directory: Error: [vitest] No "getSessionDir" export is defined on the "../../../src/services/gitmem-dir.js" mock`. The omitted export **is** reached, throws, and is caught by `cleanupSessionFiles`' own try/catch — so the tests pass while the cleanup path they execute fails. Silent failure (class A) inside the test suite itself.

| test file:line | mocked module | omitted exports reachable from the code under test | reached today? |
|---|---|---|---|
| tools/session-close-payload-path.test.ts:130 | gitmem-dir.ts | `getSessionDir` | **yes — throws, swallowed** (observed) |
| tools/session-close-supabase.test.ts:152 | gitmem-dir.ts | `getSessionDir` | **yes — same path** |
| tools/session-close-validation.test.ts:119 | gitmem-dir.ts | `getSessionDir` | **yes — same path** |
| tools/session-close-validation.test.ts:48 | session-state.ts | `resolveCurrentSession`, `getCurrentSession`, `getProject`, `getConfirmations`, `getReflections` | on paths not taken by these cases (pro close, recovery) — silent-but-live |
| tools/session-close-payload-path.test.ts:59 | session-state.ts | same five | silent-but-live |
| tools/session-close-supabase.test.ts:78 | session-state.ts | `resolveCurrentSession`, `getCurrentSession`, `getProject`, `getReflections` | silent-but-live |
| tools/session-close-bridge.test.ts:72 | session-state.ts | `resolveCurrentSession`, `getCurrentSession`, `getProject` | silent-but-live |
| tools/session-close-validation.test.ts:24 | tier.ts | `hasFullAliases` | only via definitions import; not called |
| tools/confirm-scars.test.ts:14 | session-state.ts | `getRecallFailure` | **gates the GIT-93 retrieval-failure branch** — that branch is untestable from this file |
| tools/session-start-cross-project.test.ts:17 | agent-detection.ts | `getAgentIdentity` | reachable via recall/metrics imports; not called on the free path tested |
| 17 files | supabase-client.ts | up to 25 exports each (`directQuery`, `directPatch`, `listRecords`, `getRecord`, `scarSearch`, `loadLearnings*`, `getLearningsFingerprint`…) | per file: mostly unused by the tested path; `directPatch`/`directQuery` omitted in 12/14 files means any new patch/query in the code under test fails inside a try/catch rather than the test |

**Pattern:** every session-close test builds its own partial world by hand (≈20 `vi.mock` factories each). Adding one import to `session-close.ts` silently turns part of its behaviour off in all four suites.

### 6. Top 10 most dangerous blind spots (risk × coverage)

1. **`thread-supabase.ts` sync loop (843 lines, class b):** the GIT-70 test fails only the *lookup* (`directQuery`), which propagates; it never fails the write helpers, which swallow. That is exactly the gap behind B-1/A-2 (threads reported synced and pruned when every write failed).
2. **Remote recall RPC name is pinned by a stubbed test** (`scar-search-rpc-name.test.ts`) to a function no customer has. The test protects the bug.
3. **`startup.ts` (827, class b):** `loadScarsFromSupabase` → `[]` on failure → `flushCache` empties the index and the hook cache, returns success (E-5). No test fails the network during a flush.
4. **Session-close suites (≈20 hand-built mocks each):** omitted exports are reached and swallowed today (`getSessionDir`). Any new import disables behaviour silently.
5. **`archive-learning.ts`:** tests mock the prefix query, so the `like.` on a UUID column (42883 on every customer store, verified) never reached a real PostgREST.
6. **`init-wizard.js` (1,072, no CI):** writes the store to the wrong root (D 1.1).
7. **`auto-retrieve-hook.sh`:** no test; dead on stock macOS (D 7.1).
8. **`migrate-local.ts` (360, zero):** the path that lost 20 of 23 learnings in feedback c6e4cdfc has no regression test.
9. **`embedding.ts` on Pro:** every test touching it mocks it; feedback 54cb480a (embeddings not generated) has no regression test.
10. **`server.ts` (531, zero unit):** the display-only response rule (fields never reach the agent) and tool registration at the optimistic tier are exercised only by spawned-server smoke tests on the free tier.

### What the feedback says about the classes

- 13 of the 21 bug reports are class **B (false success)**; 7 are class **A**; 5 touch **D**. Every 1.8.0-era report (rows 1–12) is B, A or D, and every one of those was fixed in 1.9.0–1.11.0. No feedback row maps to class C or E: schema and concurrency failures are not being reported by users, which is consistent with them being silent (A) rather than absent.
- Two fixed defects have **no regression test**: Pro embedding generation (#22) and `migrate-local` (#23).
- Two reports are **open by design or partially addressed**: enforcement nag on benign tools (#27), cache-health scar-only (#15), and the learnings side of #10.
- The triage columns are unused, so a report's status is not recoverable from the table itself (GIT-47 asked for an admin read/triage path).

## 11. Verification log and corrections

**Read-only probes against the venue** (`vujndhcumgroztfkkdus`, a setup.sql store; service-role key read from the local env file and never printed):

| Probe | Request | Result | Confirms |
|---|---|---|---|
| Remote recall RPC as the code calls it | `POST /rest/v1/rpc/match_gitmem_learnings_weighted` `{query_embedding, match_count, match_threshold}` | **404 PGRST202** "Searched for the function … no matches" | #1 |
| The function `setup.sql` provides | `POST /rest/v1/rpc/gitmem_scar_search` `{query_embedding, match_count, similarity_threshold}` | 400 "vector must have at least 1 dimension": the function exists (the test vector was empty) | #1's fix target |
| `archive_learning` prefix lookup | `GET /rest/v1/gitmem_learnings?select=id&id=like.abcd%25&limit=2` | **42883** "operator does not exist: uuid ~~ unknown" | #9 |
| Shape of a closed session's `open_threads` | `GET gitmem_sessions?select=open_threads…` | Elements are JSON-stringified `ThreadObject`s inside `TEXT[]` | #19 |

**Code re-reads by hand:** #2 (`init-wizard.js:169`), #3 (`auto-retrieve-hook.sh:174`, plus `command -v timeout gtimeout` → none on the audit Mac), #4 (all three helpers catch and return `null`/`false`/`void`; the GIT-70 test rejects only `directQuery`), #5 (`startup.ts:225-227, 677-690`), #8 (`active-sessions.ts:465`), #16 (`bin/gitmem.js:659, 805-807`), and the live `getSessionDir` mock omission (observed in the session-close suites' stderr during Release C).

**Corrections to sweep reports:**
- **F top-10 #10 is wrong:** `pg` **is** a dependency (`package.json:42`); `activate`'s `DATABASE_URL` path is not broken for that reason. Dropped from the ranking.
- **F #7 narrowed:** `install-hooks` is guarded when gitmem hooks are already present; it replaces *other* hooks only when no gitmem hook is found. `uninstall-hooks` deletes all hooks unconditionally.
- **C #2 re-rated:** from high to low-medium. After GIT-98's per-row delta the v1.8.0 refresh function costs a refetch of the scored rows (≈42 KB measured), not the whole index.
- **D (classes 7–9) and G (sections 3–6)** were completed by the lead auditor after the sweeps were cut off by a spend limit; they're marked as such inline.
- The class G mock-factory figures come from a static reachability analysis (upper bound); the table in G§3 narrows them by hand.

**Not verified (reported as the sweeps found them):** E's race timings (#6, #7, #8, #18) are traced through code, not reproduced. The concurrency scenarios need the Phase 2 harness to demonstrate.
